Chenbro  | Asia Pacific
August Sales Flat MoM/+41%
Y oY
In this report, we focus on Chenbro's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• August  sales were NT$2,457mn (flat MoM/+41% YoY).
• QTD sales reached 59% of our 3Q26 estimate at NT$8,368mn (+7% QoQ/
+48% YoY) and 56% of consensus at NT$8,763mn (+12% QoQ/+55% YoY).
Our view:
• We see its 3Q26 revenue tracking slightly behind our estimate.
• We think near-term sentiment should remain lackluster amid the temporary

hiccup resulting from supply chain management.
• Management remains optimistic on the 2H26 outlook. The demand from its
GPU/ASIC server clusters and general servers is strong, driving  orders for its
chassis and rack offerings.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chenbro (8210.TW)
Base case, derived from 27x 2027e P/E. We view our target multiple as justified by the 33%
earnings CAGR we see for 2025-28, which implies a PEG of 0.8. This is in line the average of
0.8 over 2014-24, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 12x for a 15% earnings
CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI capex outlook
n Larger market share gains within existing customers

n Faster penetration into new customers
n Higher entry barrier among peers that leads to higher ASP/profitability
n Better macro outlook
Risks to Downside
n Softer AI capex
n Less share gains within existing customers
n Slower penetration into new customers
n Intensifying competition leading to lower ASP/profitability
n Continuous macro uncertainties

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy ,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover . Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy , hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety , and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk -adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk -adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk -adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk -adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below .
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below .

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below .
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 129: 2张反对票意味着什么？日本这次加息“最值得玩味的问题”

Source PDF: all report pdf/2张反对票意味着什么？日本这次加息“最值得玩味的问题”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-19
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

两张鸽派反对票意外触发 “ 加息悖论 ” 。为防明年⼈事换⾎后鸽派掣肘，叠加美联储加息施
压，⽇本央⾏被迫开启 “ ⽣死时速 ” 。花旗预计其将⼤幅提速，抢在 2027 年 7 ⽉前冲顶 2% 终
端利率。
摘要：
在 2026 年 9 ⽉的议息会议上，⽇本央⾏如期将政策利率从 1.00% 上调⾄ 1.25% 。然⽽，剥开符合
预期的表象，真正的市场信号隐藏在两张反对票中。花旗银⾏指出⼀个看似⽭盾的 “ 悖论 ” ：鸽派
委员的反对票，反⽽将促使⽇本央⾏以更快的速度冲刺终端利率。
据追⻛交易台，花旗银⾏⽇本经济分析师 Sosuke Nakamura 18 ⽇发布研究报告，深度复盘⽇
本央⾏（ BoJ ） 9 ⽉议息会议。⽇本央⾏如期将政策利率从 1.00% 上调⾄ 1.25% ，但此次会议最
值得玩味之处，恰恰不在于加息本⾝，⽽在于两张反对票。
由于鹰派委员将于 2027 年 7 ⽉退休，且后续可能被鸽派接替，⽇本央⾏必须在 “ 窗⼝期 ” 关闭前完
成加息任务。叠加美联储本周意外加息的外部压⼒，花旗研究⼤幅上调加息预期节奏，将终端

利率达成时间从原先预期的 2027 年 12 ⽉提前⾄ 2027 年 7 ⽉，维持 2.00% 的终端利率预测不变，
并预计加息节点分别落在 2026 年 12 ⽉、 2027 年 3 ⽉和 2027 年 7 ⽉。
两张反对票，来⾃谁、意味着什么
⽇本央⾏ 9 ⽉会议以多数票通过加息决定，但委员浅⽥统⼀郎（ Asada ）和佐藤绫乃（ Sato ）对
本次加息投下了反对票 ——两⼈均由⾼市早苗内阁任命。这是⾃ 2025 年 6 ⽉浅⽥单独投出反对票
以来，鸽派阵营在央⾏内部最明显的⼀次集体亮相。
从历史记录来看，过去⼀年多时间⾥，反对票⼤多来⾃鹰派委员（⾼⽥创、⽥村直树、中川顺
⼦等），⽅向是 " 应该更快加息 " 。⽽此次两张反对票的⽅向恰好相反 ——" 应该维持利率不变 " 。这
⼀转变清晰表明，⾼市内阁的鸽派⽴场已通过⼈事任命渗透进央⾏决策层。
与此同时，市场此前有预期会出现 50 个基点的⼀次性⼤幅加息提案，但此事并未发⽣。综合来
看，本次会议结果略偏鸽派，低于市场中性预期。
悖论：反对票反⽽加速了终端利率的到来

花旗报告指出这⼀看似⽭盾的逻辑：两张反对票虽然提⾼了明年 7 ⽉之后继续加息的⻔槛，却同
时加快了⽇本央⾏在此之前完成加息周期的紧迫感。
逻辑链条如下：
鹰派委员⾼⽥创和⽥村直树预计于 2027 年 7 ⽉离任。若⾼市内阁届时任命⽴场相近于浅⽥、
佐藤的鸽派接替者，央⾏内部 9 名委员中鸽派数量最多可达 4 ⼈；
4 ⽐ 5 的投票格局虽然在数学上不妨碍加息，但这并⾮⽇本央⾏偏好的决策⽅式，且现有中⽴
委员也存在随加息推进⽽转向鸽派的⻛险；
因此，在鹰派委员仍在任期间完成加息，是更为审慎的策略选择；
两张反对票发出的信号，让这种 " 抢在⼈事变动前完成加息 " 的逻辑更具说服⼒。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

基于此，花旗将终端利率的预期达成时点从此前的 2027 年 12 ⽉⼤幅提前⾄ 2027 年 7 ⽉，加息节
点调整为： 2026 年 12 ⽉、 2027 年 3 ⽉、 2027 年 7 ⽉，终端利率维持 2.00% 不变。
美联储加息是关键变量，⽇本央⾏被迫跟进
花旗的原有预测假设美联储今年余下时间维持利率不变，并据此预计⽇本央⾏下次加息将在
2027 年 1 ⽉。然⽽，美联储本周实际选择了加息 —— 与花旗此前的基准预期相悖。
这⼀变化直接影响⽇本央⾏的加息路径。报告指出，为缓解⽇美利差对⽇元造成的贬值压⼒，
⽇本央⾏需要在节奏上跟上乃⾄超越美联储。具体⽽⾔：
若美联储按其点阵图中值于年内再度加息，⽇本央⾏很可能被迫于 12 ⽉将利率升⾄ 1.50% ；
随后在 2027 年 3 ⽉进⼀步升⾄ 1.75% ；
考虑到⽇本 2027 年 4 ⽉将实施⻝品消费税削减，届时物价将受到向下压⼒，在此之前完成⼀
次加息在策略上更为合理。
中东局势亦是重要变量 —— 若地缘紧张持续推⾼能源价格，主要央⾏的鹰派取向将延续，⽇本

央⾏跟进加息的概率将随之上升；反之，若局势趋稳、能源价格回落，紧迫性将有所降低。
⽇元加息之后仍然⾛弱
9 ⽉ 18 ⽇当天，尽管⽇本央⾏完成加息，⽇元兑美元仍持续承压，盘中⼀度跌超 1% ，美元兑⽇
元突破 158.00 关⼝创两周新低。加息未能提振⽇元，折射出市场真正关注的问题 —— 后续加息
路径是否可持续。
据⽇本媒体报道，⽇本央⾏当⽇在外汇市场向参与者询问汇率⽔平，即进⾏ " 汇率摸底 " 。这⼀消
息传出后，⽇元跌幅有所收窄，美元兑⽇元回落⾄ 156.80 附近。美国银⾏外汇策略师 Alex
Cohen 将此形容为 " 向市场发出的⼜⼀次警告 " ，彭博宏观策略师 Brendan Fagan 则指出，这⼀举
措将使交易员在追逐⽇元贬值、尤其是汇率接近 160 时有所顾虑。
值得注意的是，汇率摸底并不等于实际⼲预。⽇本政府今年 7 ⽉底曾与美国联合实施买⼊⽇元⼲
预， 7 ⽉ 30 ⽇⾄ 8 ⽉ 26 ⽇期间合计投⼊ 15.4 万亿⽇元，创下单⽉⼲预⾦额历史新⾼。但在⽇美利
差格局未发⽣根本改变的情况下，⼲预能否带来持续的⽇元升值，仍存在较⼤疑问。

植⽥和男⾏⻓当⽇表⽰政策制定已进⼊ " 新阶段 " ，未排除未来加息可能，但同时坦⾔当前紧缩周
期的终端利率⽔平 " 难以判断 " 。德意志银⾏⽇本固定收益策略主管⼤森翔希评论称： " 植⽥和男
的⼯作⼏乎已经困难到了极点。没有任何⼀种政策节奏能够同时令投资者、华盛顿和⽇本政府
满意。 "
通胀与实体经济：双重约束下的加息空间
在基本⾯⽅向上，花旗预计消费者价格指数（ CPI ）将持续上⾏⾄ 2027 年第⼀季度，这为年内及
明年初的加息决策提供了相对充分的依据。明年春季 Shunto 劳资谈判开始之际，通胀预计仍将
⾼于当前⽔平，这将⽀持薪资保持较强增⻓势头。
然⽽， 2027 年 4 ⽉⻝品消费税削减措施实施后， CPI 可能跌回 2% 以下，届时以通胀为由继续加息
的叙事逻辑将显著弱化 —— 即便剔除税率调整影响后通胀仍⾼于 2% ，其从第⼀季度峰值回落的
趋势也将给后续加息制造障碍。

### Page 3

实体经济层⾯同样存在隐患：即便实际⼯资已转正，私⼈消费依然低迷；即便有 AI 相关需求⽀
撑，资本⽀出仍显疲软。  报告指出，⽇本消费者和企业在漫⻓的通缩岁⽉中已形成深刻的⾏为
惯性，⼀旦政策利率升⾄某⼀临界点，经济可能出现急剧放缓。这使得终端利率显著突破 2.00%
的可能性相对有限。
花旗认为，在政策利率升⾄ 1.75% （接近⽇本央⾏中性利率区间中点）之后，进⼀步加息的节奏
将在参考实体经济、汇率⽔平和股票价格的基础上进⾏校准，未来未必会延续每季度⼀次的快
速推进模式。
对投资者意味着什么？
综合以上分析，当前⽇本利率交易的核⼼⽭盾在于：加息路径的前端（ 2026 年底⾄ 2027 年上半
年）被压缩提速，⽽终端利率的上限（ 2.00% ）却因鸽派压⼒⽽未⻅打开。
对于投资者⽽⾔，这⼀格局意味着：
⽇元短期⽀撑有限，利差驱动的贬值压⼒仍在，汇率⼲预⻛险值得关注，尤其在汇率接近 160
附近；
⽇本国债收益率曲线短端承压，加息节奏前移将使 2026 年底⾄ 2027 年中期利率上⾏预期更为
集中；
⽇本股市⾯临双重压⼒：⽇元过快贬值对出⼝企业有利，但快速加息与⽇元快速升值的潜在
⻛险均可能对权益估值形成冲击；
⾼市内阁⼈事安排与财政政策⾛向，是 2027 年中期之后⽇本货币政策最⼤的⾮经济变量，其
影响或将在明年 7 ⽉之后逐步显现。

Document 239: Consumer Checkpoint_ Still sizzling August spending remained robust as consumers sought value, income groups converged and household finances stayed healthy. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Still sizzling August spending remained robust as consumers sought value, income groups converged and household finances stayed healthy. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13017580     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling 
14 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained solid in August. Total card spending per household rose 0.9% month-over-month and 4.5% year-
over-year, according to Bank of America internal data. Discretionary services continue to provide a steady foundation for this 
growth, despite moderation in a few categories. 
• Consumers still appear to be focused on value. Spending at general merchandise and big-box retailers continues to outperform 
more traditional retailers, as evidenced during the back-to-school season. This suggests households are actively seeking lower-
cost alternatives while maintaining spending. 
• Spending and wage growth have largely converged across income groups, though across generations, the largest "K" shape 
remains among Millennials. Household finances also remain healthy, with credit card utilization declining across most age and 
income cohorts. Lower- and middle-income households still hold elevated deposit balances across all age groups, suggesting 
spending should remain resilient.

A summer of spending  
Consumer spending looked very robust in August. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household jumped 0.9% month-over-month (MoM) on a seasonally-adjusted basis, following the 0.2% dip in July. And card 
spending rose 4.5% year-over-year (YoY), more than four times the average growth rate of 2025. Excluding gasoline, spending 
was up 3.7% YoY, almost 2.5 times the rate in 2025. Additionally, while August YoY growth rates have eased over the past two 
months, they remained above the first three months of 2026 (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 4.5% YoY in August and 3.7% YoY excluding gasoline, with seasonally-adjusted spending up 0.9%

Consumers continue to pivot to big-box stores and wholesalers 
One “stand-out” among discretionary spending categories is general merchandise, where robust and rising transaction growth 
has largely mirrored its spending growth, up 4.7% and 5.6% YoY, respectively.   
In our view, this partly reflects consumers searching for value to offset the increase in gas prices, among other items (read more 
in The rise of value). This trend extends to back-to-school spending. In fact, while spending at traditional back-to-school retailers 
(like teen and children’s apparel) has declined this year, outlays have ramped up at general merchandise stores (Exhibit 6). This 
suggests to us that back-to-school spending was likely solid this year but just shifted to different channels.

Spending and wages keep converging, but it differs slightly by age 
Looking across income cohorts, total card spending growth has largely converged in recent months, effectively closing the “K” 
shape in overall spending (Exhibit 7). This is also true in discretionary spending: Bank of America card data shows lower-income 
households’ discretionary spending growth is recovering strongly. In August, discretionary spending rose 5.9% YoY for higher-
income households versus 5.7% YoY for lower-income counterparts, the smallest gap since January 2024 (Exhibit 8). Meanwhile, 
middle-income households’ discretionary spending also improved but lagged other income cohorts slightly, albeit still up a 
healthy 5.1% YoY.  
The closing gap in spending growth between higher- and lower-income households has been mirrored in strengthening after-tax 
wage growth (read more in The Institute Employment Report: August 2026). Lower-income households' wage growth outpaced 
that of other cohorts in August for the second consecutive month (Exhibit 9). 
Exhibit 7: Lower-income households’ total card spending growth is

While the “K-shaped” divergence in discretionary credit and debit card spending has closed, it likely persists in higher-cost items 
and services such as vehicles and moving (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?).  
We see two reasons for this. First, these types of purchases may require saving for down payments or larger borrowing (and may 
be more interest rate-sensitive), so it’s possible recent wage growth has yet to translate into bigger-ticket buys. Second, some 
of the improvement in after-tax wage growth may be due to temporary factors like lower tax withholdings, meaning some 
consumers may not feel comfortable signing up for higher-dollar and longer-term financial commitments.  
Gen Z, Millennials and Gen X have less room to borrow, but elevated deposits 
When it comes to overall financial health, we believe consumers remain on solid footing. One sign of this is that more consumers 
are paying off their entire credit card balance every month, both compared to last year and to pre-pandemic levels (read more in 
Consumer Checkpoint: The great convergence). And for those who do carry a balance (revolvers) from month to month, overall 
utilization rates have declined YoY and are near pre-pandemic levels (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: Credit card utilization for revolvers is on par with pre-pandemic levels and has declined YoY 
Median credit card utilization for credit card revolvers (monthly, index 2019 average = 100, below 100 means there has been a decrease relative to 2019 levels)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at utilization rates for revolvers by age and income, Baby Boomers have more room to borrow than younger generations 
across all income cohorts. Even in August 2026, Gen Z, Millennial and Gen X utilization rates are still well above Baby Boomer’s 
2019 utilization rates (Exhibit 12). The good news is that most of these younger generations have seen borrowing capacity 
increase slightly since then. Millennials, however, at the margin have made the least progress, with utilization rates still at 2019 
levels.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 247: Dutch pension funds no longer a shock absorber of higher rates

Source PDF: all report pdf/Dutch pension funds no longer a shock absorber of higher rates.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 21 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  21 September 2026   RATES   THE NETHERLANDS
Dutch pension funds no longer a shock
absorber of higher rates
Dutch pension funds once provided a natural source of demand as rates
rose. The new pension framework offers less flexibility, making a similar
wave of receiver-swap buying less likely. We still see demand for credit,
mainly in assets that closely track euro swaps

Dutch pension reforms are changing how funds manage risk and respond to rising rates
Pension funds won’t jump in to absorb the rise in rates under
new system
Under the old system, pension funds often had greater flexibility in managing liability hedges,
triggering large flows into euro fixed receiver swaps when rates rose. This was particularly
noticeable after 2021, when interest coverage ratios rose by some 30 percentage points.
Pension funds had reduced their hedges during the low-rate environment and increased them

significantly again when rates rose on the back of higher inflation. This helped to lock in
healthy funding ratios.

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 21 September 2026 2
Pension funds increased interest rate hedges when rates and
funding ratios rose
Source: ING, DNB, Macrobond
The new system does not offer the same dynamic hedging flexibility, which also means
pension funds will be less opportunistic when rates rise. Instead, the interest rate hedging
ratios per age cohort are predetermined in each fund’s transition plans. For instance, ABP (the
largest fund with €500bn assets), sets a 10% hedging ratio for younger participants, which

then rises to 75% for those in retirement. In theory, these could be adjusted at later stages,
but doing so is more complex than under the old system.
More static hedging and structurally lower demand for longer-dated rates add to the upward
pressure on 30Y euro swap rates. Liquidity conditions at the long-end of the curve can also
deteriorate, something we’ve already observed in recent market moves. For this reason, we
continue to see steepening pressures on the 10s30s curve, although much hinges on the

global macro environment. A flattening of the 5s10s on the back of higher oil prices pushes
against these forces.
Most pension funds are still incentivised to track the EURIBOR
curve
Whilst the new system no longer relies on a funding ratio, the EURIBOR 6M curve remains an
important benchmark for hedging purposes. So even though we see scope to rotate away
from government bonds to credit, we expect funds to remain focused on credit products that
closely track the swap curve.

The old system did not distinguish between individual returns – everything went into one big
pot – which was easy to report and communicate to participants. Better portfolio returns
would simply translate into a higher funding ratio. All liabilities were also hedged together. If
the portfolio of hedges did not follow the regulatory discount rate closely (i.e. the EURIBOR 6M
swap curve), the difference would simply be captured in the overall return of the portfolio. A

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 21 September 2026 3
relative gain due to, for example, positive swap spreads, would benefit all participants equally.
In the new system, interest rate hedging is applied by age cohort, and consists of a “protection
return” and an “excess return” component. In practice, this still means funds manage two
portfolios, but the allocation of each portfolio’s returns changes over a participant’s life cycle.

Young participants are almost entirely exposed to the excess return whereas those in
retirement are mostly exposed to the protection return.
The protection return can be calculated using the theoretical returns from a EURIBOR swap or
from a dedicated hedging portfolio. Most funds seem to choose the theoretical method but still
construct a hedging portfolio that includes a mix of bonds and swaps. This leads to a mismatch
between the actual return of the hedging portfolio and the protection return allocated to

participants. The mismatch is added to the excess return component, which means that young
participants will mostly absorb this.
Mismatches with the swap curve get allocated to younger
participants
Source: ING
The mismatch can, for instance, stem from sharp credit spread moves and can be difficult to
explain to younger participants. Young participants might find themselves penalised by losses
in the hedging portfolio, even though those investments are primarily for older participants. To

avoid communication challenges, pension funds will therefore still be incentivised to construct
a hedging portfolio that closely tracks the swap curve.
Credit to benefit, but only those trading in line with euro swaps
Whilst we anticipate a rotation away from government bonds towards corporate credit for the
hedging portfolio, credit quality will remain important. Government bonds no longer benefit
from preferential regulatory treatment and the shortening of duration will also help other

credit products. Therefore we anticipate an increase in demand for investment grade and
Supranational, Subsovereign and Agency (SSA) bonds. But at the same time, we acknowledge
credits with more volatile spreads versus swap curves will look less attractive to hold for
hedging purposes. The latest data already confirms a gradual rotation from government
bonds to corporate credit.

### Page 4

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 21 September 2026 4
In addition, as the new system offers a clearer separation of risk for younger and older
participants, we could see less demand for assets with a more moderate risk profile. Pension
funds can increase the exposure to riskier assets without negatively impacting older
participants. As such, categories such as USD bonds and emerging market hard currency debt
might see a structural fall in demand.

Corporate credit benefits from the reforms, at the expense of
govies
Source: ING, DNB
Pension funds will be forced to rebalance hedges more
frequently
Moving from a defined benefits (DB) to defined contribution (DC) system also changes the
liabilities base for hedges, forcing more frequent rebalancing. Liabilities under the old system
were relatively stable since these were promised payments in the future. In the new system,
the liabilities are a direct function of the fund’s total assets, which is volatile by nature and

moves up and down alongside global financial markets.
When equities rise in value, pension funds will have to increase interest rate hedges to match
higher liabilities. In theory, this should trigger large swap flows on a monthly basis, to assure
compliance with the set hedging ratios. In practice, however, pension funds will likely operate
with narrow ranges around the hedging targets. While this should avoid sharp flows on every
market move, we still anticipate more activity than under the old system.

Document 300: MEGA II - Not done yet (2026-09-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/MEGA II - Not done yet (2026-09-16).pdf

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank Research Institute
Deutsche Bank

### Page 2

Maximilian Uleer, Carolin Raab, Francesca Mazzali | Equity Outlook | September 2026
Europe is back and Germany’s potential
seems not yet fully appreciated by markets
In early 2025, we made a bold call: we argued that Europe’s 15-year history of underperformance has come to an end (MEGA and here). We
projected the P/E valuation gap between US and European equities to narrow from a record 9.0x at the end of 2024 to 4.3x. Since then, the
gap has indeed tightened to 4.7x. With Germany’s potential still being underestimated by markets, we expect more to come for Europe.

Looking ahead, we see several structural factors supporting Europe’s relative attractiveness:
– Valuations: After the tightening, we expect the gap to stabilize. Higher US growth justifies a premium but most of the below arguments
argue against a renewed widening.
– Earnings: We expect the European earnings recovery to continue, delivering double-digit growth in 2026. While US earnings are still
expected to grow faster, uncertainty around the sustainability of future growth, particularly in the Tech sector, remains elevated.

– Germany: The US and China are likely to continue growing faster than Europe, but much of that growth has been accompanied by
widening deficits and rising debt. US fiscal deficit and debt metrics now exceed European levels. At the same time, Germany’s fiscal pivot
could support a relative recovery in European growth, an opportunity that equity markets have yet to fully embrace. A recovery in global
manufacturing activity provides an additional boost to Europe’s economy. Germany could become the powertrain of Europe’s economic

recovery. Having reduced its debt-to-GDP ratio over the past decade despite macro headwinds, it now has the fiscal capacity to support
growth through a substantial spending plan including EUR 1tr in new debt. The economy is showing greater resilience than many expected,
manufacturing activity is improving, international investor interest is beginning to re-emerge and reforms are advancing. We estimate the
pension reform alone could unlock €10-15bn of annual inflows into European equities.

– Diversification: Europe’s equity market is well diversified across sectors, providing broader return drivers than more concentrated markets
such as the US and Emerging Markets. This helps cushion performance during sharp market downturns.
– AI: While Europe is unlikely to catch up with the leading AI developers, it is also less exposed to risks surrounding the monetisation of heavy
AI investment. Instead, it should benefit indirectly from AI spending through its advanced manufacturing capabilities, its exposure to AI

infrastructure and through growing AI adoption itself.
– Political stability: While Europe’s fragmented political landscape is a structural disadvantage, external pressures from US and Chinese
trade policies and ongoing geopolitical challenges have strengthened coordination across the EU on trade, defence and fiscal matters.

### Page 13

Deep dive: Germany

### Page 14

Maximilian Uleer, Carolin Raab, Francesca Mazzali | Equity Outlook | September 2026
Germany has the potential to become the driving force behind Europe’s economic revival. Despite years of macro headwinds, it
reduced its debt ratio over the past decade, creating the fiscal space for a major spending package. Economic activity is
stabilising, manufacturing conditions are improving, reforms are progressing, and global investor interest is returning. Germany

has repeatedly demonstrated an ability to adapt and reinvent itself, with major reform waves following the coalition collapses
of 1966 and 1982 and the implementation of Agenda 2010 in the early 2000s.
– Fiscal: Few countries have faced as many challenges as Germany in recent years: rising Chinese competition in key industries,
the energy shock following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine, large-scale immigration, US tariffs, and a three-year manufacturing

downturn. Despite these headwinds, Germany is the only G7 economy to have reduced its debt-to-GDP ratio over the past
decade. The government plans to raise around €1tn of additional debt by 2030, on top of the current debt stock of €2.8tn. Net
credit borrowing is expected to increase from €180bn in 2026 to more than €200bn in 2027, while spending on defence and
investment is set to reach roughly €120bn each this year, up more than 30% year-on-year.

– Macro: Germany's economy is showing greater resilience towards the ongoing geopolitical headwinds than expected. The
manufacturing PMI climbed above 54 in August, its strongest reading in over four years, and both the Ifo and ZEW surveys
surprised positively. Factory orders are gradually improving and GDP growth in H1 exceeded expectations, prompting
consensus GDP forecasts to be revised up. Although higher energy prices may create short-term headwinds, the burden

remains manageable, with energy costs representing just slightly more than 1% of sales for listed German companies.
– Politics: Germany's reform momentum is strengthening. The government is combining fiscal stimulus with measures to lower
energy costs, boost corporate investment, accelerate infrastructure projects, reform the labour market, and modernise the
pension system. We estimate the pension reform alone could unlock €60-100bn of annual capital market inflows. At the same

time, political risks appear manageable. Regional elections are unlikely to affect federal policymaking, and we see little risk to
the government's reform agenda. If anything, weaker results for the coalition parties could increase pressure to accelerate
reforms.
– Equities: Markets have yet to price in Germany's improving outlook. While economic indicators continue to strengthen, investor
flows into German equities remain weak and the MDAX has lagged the DAX by 7% since late June. As the index with the

greatest exposure to the domestic economy, the MDAX is the purest listed play on Germany's recovery and has historically
shown a much stronger relationship with German GDP growth. A combination of fiscal stimulus, structural reforms, and more
industry-friendly policies creates substantial catch-up potential following years of underperformance. Importantly, valuations
remain attractive, with the MDAX trading at its cheapest relative level in history.

Don’t underestimate Germany’s
capacity to change

– German debt-to-GDP dropped by 8 percentage

### Page 18

Maximilian Uleer, Carolin Raab, Francesca Mazzali | Equity Outlook | September 2026
Key takeaways
BIG PICTURE: Consolidation to expansion
– The German government plans to increase debt by more
than €1 trillion by 2030, coming from €2.8 trillion in 2025
(gross general government debt). Almost 70% of the debt
increase will be related to the core budget, vs 30% in off-
budget funds.
– A large share of the additional debt (>€600bn) will be
allocated to defence and security-related spending, almost

€300bn to the special infrastructure fund (especially
transportation and digitalization), and the remaining amount
to other core-budget borrowing.
– For more information, see here.

Key takeaways
Regional elections
– Contrary to many concerns, the Saxony-Anhalt election

triggered little to no market reaction. While the AfD will likely
enter government, the economic relevance of Saxony-Anhalt
within Germany is limited. In Meck-Pom and Berlin, AfD
support is weaker, though fragmented parliaments may
complicate coalition building.
– Regional elections have limited implications for federal
politics. More importantly, we don’t see the elections
derailing the government's reform agenda. If anything,
weaker results for coalition parties could increase pressure to

accelerate reforms.

### Page 32

Germany: Reform hammer in full swing
Area Detailed measures Status
Health
Care
System
– Restricting free family insurance
– Higher contributions by higher earners
– More co-payments by patients and less
benefits (braces, dentures, homeopathy)
– Price caps for hospitals/pharma
– Approved in
parliament in July,
– Effective in
2027/2028
Private
Pension
System
– “Frühstart-Rente” (early start pension):

monthly public grant of €10/child (ages 6-
18), to be invested into capital market
products, tax-free until the payout phase.
Withdrawals generally allowed from age 65.
– Approved by the
Cabined in
August,
Parliamentary
approval pending
Statutory
Pension
System
– Gradual increase in retirement age to 67.5
– Limiting early retirement programs
– Age for old-age part-time work up to 58
– Raising number of contributors, e.g. self-
employed, MPs, managers

– Introduction of mandatory funded
component: 2% additional contribution into
centralized pension fund.
– Approved by
coalition
committee,
Parliamentary
approval pending
Labour
Market
– Easier extension of fixed-term contracts
– relaxed dismissal protection for top earners
– Preferential tax treatment if a new job is
taken up quickly
– Medical certificate from first day of illness
– Approved by
coalition
committee,
Parliamentary
approval pending
Income

Tax
– Increase of basic tax-free allowance
– Higher deductions for families and
employees
– Flattening of the tax rate for low income
– Increase of “rich-tax” to 45% on taxable
income >€250k (47% for income >€280k)
– Approved by the
Cabinet in
September,
Parliamentary

### Page 33

Maximilian Uleer, Carolin Raab, Francesca Mazzali | Equity Outlook | September 2026
Key takeaways
Germany: pension reform
– The planned reform of Germany’s pension system could
generate significant inflows into European equities by
shifting retirement savings towards capital markets.
– On the private pension side, the new “old-age provision
account” encourages savers to move funds from low-yield
deposits and insurance products into higher-return
investments. If half of the eligible population invests the

annual amount required to receive the full tax benefit
(€2,300), inflows could reach €50bn per year.
– We also expect assets to migrate from the existing “Riester”
system. With total estimated Riester assets of €150bn, a 10%
annual reallocation would generate a further €15bn of
inflows.
– In parallel, the government plans to introduce a funded
component to the statutory pension system, equivalent to 2
pp of gross salaries, to be invested through a central public

pension fund. Contributions are planned to rise from 0.5pp in
2028 to 2pp by 2031, implying annual inflows of €8bn
initially and €30-35bn by 2031, according to our economists.
– Overall, we estimate these reforms could generate €60-
100bn of additional annual capital market inflows, of which
around €10-15bn (15%) may find its way into European
equities. To put this into context: the top 50 largest
European ETFs saw inflows of c. €40bn in 2025.

Key takeaways
German reforms: example from the past
– In 2023, Germany introduced the Onshore Wind
Act and revised the Offshore Wind Energy Act to
accelerate the energy transition and streamline
renewable energy development.
– For the first time, federal states face legally
binding requirements to allocate land for wind
power. The share of Germany's land designated for

onshore wind is set to increase to 1.4% by 2027
and 2.0% by 2032.
– Policy reforms are already delivering results. In H1
2025, Germany approved 20.8 GW of new onshore
wind capacity, nearly four times the level seen in
2022, marking a record pace of expansion.

### Page 40

Maximilian Uleer, Carolin Raab, Francesca Mazzali | Equity Outlook | September 2026
Key takeaways
German reforms: example from the past
– Facing stagnant growth and rising unemployment, Germany
launched a series of labour-market reforms between 2002
and 2005, beginning with the Hartz Commission and
culminating in Agenda 2010.
– The reform package tightened unemployment benefits,
expanded labour-market flexibility, improved job placement,
and reduced income and corporate taxes to strengthen

incentives for work and investment.
– While initially controversial, the reforms helped restore
confidence: GDP growth turned positive in 2004, consumer
sentiment improved, and equity markets rebounded.

Document 319: Restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate_ mechanics and possible implications (2026-09-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate_ mechanics and possible implications (2026-09-20).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate: mechanics and
possible implications
• The next general election will take place on
7 November. Current polling is very tight and thus
clients are understandably interested in how
economic policy settings might evolve should there
be a change in Government.
• If elected to lead the next Government, the
Labour Party has pledged to restore the RBNZ's
dual mandate, requiring monetary policy to

achieve price stability while supporting maximum
sustainable employment.
• Reinstating the dual mandate would require
amendments to the Reserve Bank of New Zealand
Act 2021 and the issuance of a new Remit by the
Minister of Finance.
• The legislative process could be completed
relatively quickly, with the National Party-led
Government demonstrating in 2023 that significant
mandate changes can be enacted within weeks.
• For demand-driven economic cycles, a dual

mandate would likely have little impact on OCR
decisions, as inflation and employment objectives
generally move in the same direction.
• The main difference would emerge during supply
shocks, such as that occurring at present, where
inflation rises even as economic activity and
employment are weakened, forcing policymakers
to balance competing objectives.
• We believe a restored dual mandate could result
in a slightly slower return of inflation to target,

a slower rise in or slightly lower peak OCR and
slightly greater tolerance of temporary inflation
overshoots when labour market conditions
are weak.
One of the more significant economic policy proposals
heading into the General Election on 7 November is
the Labour Party’s proposal to restore the RBNZ’s dual
inflation and employment mandate. The proposal would
reverse one of the first legislative changes made by the
National Party-led Government after taking office in

late 2023, when it removed the objective of supporting
maximum sustainable employment and returned
the RBNZ to the prior single statutory objective of
price stability.
Current polling is very tight and thus clients are
understandably interested in how economic policy
settings might evolve should there be a change in
Government. In this article we discuss what would need
to be done to give effect to the Labour Party’s proposed
policy change, together with the more important question

of whether a return to dual objectives would materially
alter future monetary policy decisions.
What is the Labour Party proposing?
The Labour Party has indicated it would restore the
monetary policy framework that existed between 2018
and 2023. Under that framework, the RBNZ's Monetary
Policy Committee (MPC) was required to formulate policy
in a manner consistent with two economic objectives:
• achieving and maintaining stability in the general level

of prices; and
• supporting maximum sustainable employment.
It is worth emphasising that the concept of “maximum
sustainable employment” is distinct from “maximum
employment”. That is, it is recognised that there
will always be some level of unemployment or
underutilisation in a dynamic economy, and that the level
of unemployment consistent with stable inflation will also
16 Sep 2026 | Westpac NZ Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz

ECONOMIC BULLETIN

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
depend on frictional and structural factors impacting the
labour market.
The dual mandate was introduced in 2018 through
amendments to the former Reserve Bank Act and
subsequently carried over into the Reserve Bank of New
Zealand Act 2021. In December 2023, the National Party-
led Government removed the employment objective,
restoring a sole focus on price stability. Therefore, a
future Labour Party-led Government would largely be

reinstating a framework that was already in place prior to
the current Government's reforms.
At this stage, it is not clear whether there would be any
tweaks to the prior framework, such as seeking to express
the employment goal as a numerical target. However,
we think that both a future Government and the RBNZ
would be reluctant to go down this route. There is a high
degree of uncertainty about what constitutes “maximum
sustainable employment”, and conceptually this can

change over time depending on other factors (including
other economic policies) impacting the operation of the
labour market. For this reason, the RBNZ has historically
emphasized that the concept of “maximum sustainable
employment” is difficult to capture in a single indicator,
and there has been no presumption that the RBNZ can
influence factors such as the equilibrium employment
rate or labour force participation rate.
What legislative changes would be required?

Restoring the dual mandate would require both legislative
change and a new Remit to provide operational guidance
to the MPC.
The primary step would be amending the Reserve
Bank of New Zealand Act 2021. The 2023 legislation
removed the employment objective from the Act and
established price stability as the sole economic objective
of monetary policy. A future Labour Government
would need to introduce an amendment bill reversing
those changes and reinstating "supporting maximum

sustainable employment" as a statutory objective.
Because the previous framework already existed, the
legislative drafting exercise would likely be relatively
straightforward. In practical terms, Parliament could
simply restore much of the wording that existed prior to
the 2023 amendment.
Following the removal of the dual mandate in 2023, the
Remit was amended to remove references to maximum
sustainable employment while retaining the inflation
objective of keeping CPI inflation between 1% and 3%,

with a focus on the 2% midpoint over the medium term. A
Labour Government would need to issue a revised Remit
alongside the legislative amendments.
The revised Remit would likely retain the existing inflation
target band; restore references to maximum sustainable
employment; require the MPC to assess labour market
conditions alongside inflation outcomes; and explain
how policy decisions balance the two objectives. It
is worth noting that the RBNZ already monitors and

reports on many labour market variables (see, for
example, the “heatmap” depicted in Figure 2.9 of the
September 2026 Monetary Policy Statement). Under
the current framework, contributing to its assessment
of how the overall economy is tracking relative to its
productive potential.
How quickly could these changes occur?
Once a new Government is formed, experience suggests
the process could move rapidly. The National-led
Government was officially sworn in on 27 November

2023. A week later Cabinet agreed to remove the RBNZ’s
dual mandate. An amendment bill was introduced on
12 December 2023, the same day that a revised Remit
was issued by the Minister of Finance. This bill was
passed under urgency and received Royal Assent on 19
December 2023 before coming into force (along with the
Remit) a day later. Thus, the whole process was complete
just over three weeks after the new Government
took office.
A future Labour Party-led Government could potentially

move at a similar pace if it regarded restoration of the
dual mandate as a priority (whether it would be a priority
is unclear at this stage). Achieving fully legislated change
before the Christmas holiday break will be much more
difficult than in 2023, given that this year’s election is
three weeks later than that which took place in 2023, and
so official results will not be released until 27 November.
It is also unclear how long it would take to form a new

Labour Party-led Government. However, by law, the last
day for the new Parliament to sit is 14 January 2027 – six
weeks after the return of the writ on 3 December. So,
while unlikely, if Parliament was to sit in mid-January, the
new arrangements could theoretically be in place ahead
of the MPC’s first meeting next year, which is scheduled to
take place on 10 February.
Given the timeframes above, the MPC meeting on 9
December this year will almost certainly take place under

the current legislation and Remit. That said, knowledge
of impending changes could conceivably colour an
MPC member’s view at that meeting if the change was
already progressing, or about to progress, through the
legislative process.
Would a dual mandate produce different
interest rate outcomes?
The practical impact of restoring the dual mandate
depends heavily on the type of economic shock
confronting policymakers.
Historically, cycles in the New Zealand economy have

been dominated by demand shocks. In the case of
demand-driven cycles, a dual mandate will likely produce

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
little change in monetary policy decisions. This is
because when demand is running too strongly, inflation
tends to rise and the labour market tends to tighten
simultaneously. In this situation, both objectives point
in the same direction. A rise in the OCR will act to both
lower inflation and ease pressure in the labour market.
Conversely, when demand weakens, inflation pressures
fall and unemployment rises. A decline in the OCR will act

to both prevent an undue decline in inflation and a rise
in unemployment.
In these circumstances, price stability and maximum
sustainable employment are largely complementary
objectives rather than competing ones. This is one reason
why monetary policy outcomes under the dual-mandate
framework between 2018 and 2023 were not obviously
different from what might have occurred under a single
mandate. Indeed, the RBNZ delivered one of the most
aggressive tightening cycles in its history between 2021

and 2023 as inflation surged (the latter, initially due
to the supply shock that accompanied the pandemic,
but later in response to the aggressive monetary and
fiscal policy easing that occurred in New Zealand and
most elsewhere). In these circumstances, employment
considerations did not prevent substantial OCR increases
because both high inflation and historically tight labour
market indicators pointed toward the need for tighter
monetary policy.

The biggest practical differences arise during supply
shocks, such as the spike in global energy prices (and
related products) that we are presently seeing because
of the Middle East conflict (major trade disruptions,
climate-related events and pandemics fall into the
same category). Supply shocks often raise inflation
while simultaneously reducing economic activity and
employment. Under a single inflation mandate, the
MPC's primary focus is restoring price stability. While

policymakers would still consider broader economic
conditions – including the longstanding secondary
objective of avoiding unnecessary volatility in the real
economy and financial conditions – the statutory
objective clearly prioritises inflation control. In the
past the RBNZ has rightly emphasised that there is
no long run trade-off between the labour market and
inflation. Although there may be scope, depending on
circumstances, for some shorter run trade-offs that could

be exploited that see inflation deviating from target for a
bit longer to smooth the impact on the labour market.
Under a dual mandate, however, policymakers would
be formally required to consider the employment
consequences of their actions alongside the inflation
outlook. They would need to seek balance in
recommending policy actions that whilst helping to
achieve one objective, might be detrimental to achieving
the other. In practice, this could mean greater tolerance

of temporary inflation overshoots; a slower pace of policy
tightening; a longer horizon for returning inflation to
target; and therefore, a somewhat lower peak in the OCR
than would otherwise be the case.
What would it mean for the OCR outlook in
the current context?
In normal economic conditions, the practical implications
are likely to be very modest. Central banks, including
the RBNZ, recognise that low and stable inflation is a
prerequisite for sustained employment growth over

the longer term. Consequently, most monetary policy
decisions do not involve sharp trade-offs between
inflation and employment objectives and there is
certainly no trade-off in the long run.
But in the current context, with the unemployment rate
at an 11-year high, an explicit dual mandate would likely
encourage the MPC to place greater weight on potential
employment outcomes than would be the case under
the current single mandate. This might lead the MPC to

tolerate a slightly longer horizon for returning inflation to
target, leading to a slightly lower peak in the OCR than
would otherwise be the case (but a rate that would likely
remain elevated for longer given the slower adjustment to
the inflation shock).
However, in practice things may not be that clear. For
short-lived supply shocks, there’s a clear case for looking
through the inflation and reducing pressure on the
labour market. However, recently we have been in an

environment of more frequent supply shocks. If inflation
expectations are high or rising the RBNZ might still
conclude that some labour market slack is required to
ensure inflation returns to target – even over an elongated
timeframe. In the current case, it could be the case that
the MPC might be encouraged to merely slow the pace
at which they reduce stimulus/introduce restrictive
conditions if they are uncertain about how quickly the
labour market will return to a necessarily poorly defined

estimate of “maximum sustainable employment”.
The views recently expressed by clients seem to reflect
the idea that the peak OCR could be reduced under a
dual mandate in the current circumstances. In our recent
RBNZ pulse survey, half of respondents thought that
the reintroduction of the ‘dual mandate’ would lead
to a moderately (25-50bps) lower peak in the OCR for
the current cycle. And an informal poll during one of
our recent client functions found mixed views on what

a change of Government might mean for economic
activity next year, but around half thought that the main
impact would be higher inflation than otherwise. We
should remember that a new government might lead
to multiple shifts in policy settings, for example higher
levels of government spending or higher future minimum
wage settings.

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Finally, it is worth noting that at this stage election
polling remains tight. And it is unclear to what extent
current market pricing for the RBNZ’s policy settings in
2027 might already incorporate some allowance for the
possibility that there will be a change in Government and
the restoration of the dual mandate.

### Page 2

2 
4Q26 半導體產業展望 
BSPDN 與 VPD 升級供電架構 
結論與建議 
1. Backside Power Delivery Networks (BSPDN)於 4Q26 正式進入量產階段。BSPDN 將電
源線路由晶圓正面移至背面 ， 降低供電阻抗與壓降 ， 並釋放正面訊號布線空間 。 台積電A16
將於 4Q26 導入 Super Power Rail量產，Intel 18A 已採 PowerVia，Samsung SF2Z 則預
計 27 年導入 BSPDN，晶背供電正由技術驗證進入量產與滲透率提升階段。 
2. AI XPU 功耗與供電電流快速上升後，封裝端供電瓶頸由主機板延伸至高電流傳輸路徑。  
Vertical Power Delivery (VPD)透過將電壓調節模組由 XPU 側邊移至下方，縮短低壓大電
流的供電距離並改善損耗與供電穩定性 。ASE powerSiP 可將配線功率損耗由 12%降至 6%、
電流密度由 0.4A/mm²提升至 0.6A/mm²；Meta 亦已完成 850A AI ASIC VPD模組驗證，
顯示 VPD 正由封裝平台驗證逐步進入 AI 晶片實際導入階段。 
3. SEMICON T aiwan 2026 顯示，VPD 正由單一供電方案升級為 AI 先進封裝核心架構。隨
VRM 逐步靠近 XPU，電容及其他被動元件亦同步向封裝內整合，台積電 CoWoS-L 已導
入 eDTC， 日月光亦展示CAP Module 等方案 。 未來先進封裝價值將由RDL、TSV 與 HBM
互連，進一步延伸至供電、被動元件及散熱整合，推升 OSAT 單顆 AI 封裝價值量。 
4Q26 投資展望 
1. 展望 4Q26，AI 供電架構將由傳統主機板與晶圓正面供電，逐步轉向 BSPDN 與 VPD。台積
電(2330 TT)A16 將導入 Super Power Rail(SPR)晶背供電並於 4Q26 量產，新增晶圓接合、極薄
化、CMP、晶背蝕刻、薄膜沉積及檢測量測等製程，帶動單片晶圓設備與耗材價值提升。台股供
應鏈中，中砂(1560 TT) 受惠 CMP 製程與鑽石碟耗材需求增加，昇陽半導體(8028 TT) 則受惠設
備調機、製程驗證及量產監控所帶動的再生晶圓需求。 
2. 封裝端 4Q26 則將由 VPD 技術驗證逐步轉向客戶導入與前量產準備。隨 AI 處理器供電需求
朝 1,000A 以上發展，VPD 已成為 AI 先進封裝的重要架構。日月光投控(3711 TT)powerSiP 透
過將電壓調節模組移至 XPU 下方縮短供電路徑 ， 未來封裝價值可望由RDL、TSV 與 HBM 互連 ，
進一步延伸至電壓調節模組、矽電容、eDTC、嵌入式被動元件及散熱整合。

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告

3 
A16 於 4Q26 量產，晶背供電由技術藍圖正式進入製造端 
隨 AI 與高效能運算晶片朝更高電晶體密度與功耗發展，先進製程瓶頸已由電晶體微縮延伸至供
電架構。傳統邏輯晶片的訊號與電源皆由晶圓正面傳輸，隨製程持續微縮，金屬線寬縮小推升互
連電阻，電源線路亦與訊號線路競爭有限的布線空間，使壓降與布線壅塞逐步限制晶片效能。晶
背供電網路(BSPDN)則將電源線路移至晶圓背面，訊號仍由正面傳輸，再透過垂直導通結構將電
力送至電晶體附近，不僅可降低供電阻抗與壓降，也能釋放正面布線空間，進一步改善效能、功
耗與邏輯密度，成為 GAA 之後延續先進邏輯製程微縮的重要技術。  
BSPDN 的製程會先以一片厚度約 600 微米的矽晶圓為基底 ， 於其上方建構出約100 奈米厚度的
電晶體層，再於其上方堆疊約500 奈米厚度的訊號導線區。與傳統製程不同，此階段僅需構築訊
號導線，不再同時建立電源線。這是因為晶背供電架構中，電源分配網路將改由晶圓背面實現。
當訊號線區完成後 ， 需將整片晶圓與另一片厚度同樣為600 微米的矽晶圓進行 Wafer Bonding，
該上方晶圓即為承載晶圓(Carrier Wafer)。此步驟的目的，是在隨後進行背面加工時提供機械支
撐。由於後續將進行極端薄化，晶圓本體厚度會從 600 µm 減至僅剩電晶體層，此時原晶圓結構
已喪失剛性，若無外部支撐，無法承受設備夾持、搬運與後段製程的熱機應力。接著晶圓會進入
研磨與化學機械平坦化(CMP)階段，將原始矽基底完全去除，僅保留前段製成的電晶體層。此時
晶圓已被翻轉，即原本的背面朝上。接著透過矽穿孔技術，在電晶體底部開孔，以建立背面供電
通道，並於其上沉積 500 奈米的電源金屬層，完成晶背供電的製程。

圖一. Backside Power Delivery Networks Process

資料來源：Intel，國泰證期研究部整理

從競爭格局來看，晶背供電亦正由個別業者的差異化技術，逐步轉為全球先進邏輯製程的標準競
爭項目。Intel 已於 18A 導入 PowerVia，Samsung 亦規劃於 SF2Z 導入 BSPDN 並於 27 年進入
量產。隨台積電 A16 於 4Q26 正式量產，先進製程競爭將由過去 GAA 電晶體結構本身，進一步
延伸至晶背供電架構、製程整合能力及量產良率。研究部認為 4Q26 將是 BSPDN 由技術驗證轉
向量產驗證的重要分水嶺，27 年則有望進一步進入客戶滲透率與晶圓投片量擴張階段。

### Page 4

投資 顧問 
產業報告

4 
圖二：Backside Power Delivery Networks (BSPDN)

資料來源：IMEC、國泰證期研究部整理

VPD 縮短高電流供電路徑，powerSiP 提升 AI 封裝供電效率 
若 BSPDN 解決的是晶片內部供電問題，垂直供電(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)則聚焦於電力
進入封裝前的高電流傳輸路徑。傳統 AI 加速器通常將電壓調節模組配置於 XPU 周邊，電流需沿
主機板或封裝基板水平傳輸後再進入處理器。隨 AI XPU 功耗及瞬時電流需求持續提高，部分下
一代 AI 處理器供電需求已達數百安培甚至 1,000 安培以上，高電流將明顯放大配線損耗、供電
網路阻抗及電壓波動，供電架構因而逐步成為限制 AI 晶片效能的重要因素。VPD 的核心概念，
是將原本位於 XPU 側邊的電壓調節模組移至 XPU 正下方，使低壓、大電流的傳輸路徑由公分級
水平配線縮短至毫米級垂直路徑，進而降低線路電阻及配線損耗，並改善瞬時負載變化下的供電
穩定性。同時供電元件由 XPU 周邊移至下方後，亦可釋放封裝邊緣空間予 HBM、高速輸入輸出
及其他 Chiplet，使供電架構開始直接影響 AI 封裝設計。 
日月光投控 powerSiP 為目前 OSAT 端具代表性的 VPD 平台之一，透過系統級封裝將多級電壓
調節模組及被動元件垂直整合於 SoC 或 Chiplet 下方 ， 使電源可由封裝底部以較短路徑直接供應
至運算晶片。SEMICON T aiwan 2026 展示數據顯示，相較傳統水平並排式架構，powerSiP 可
將配線功率損耗由 12%降低至 6%，電流密度由 0.4A/mm²提高至 0.6A/mm²，封裝平面占用面
積則縮小約 25%。其技術價值主要來自縮短供電距離及提高垂直整合程度，而非僅依賴電源晶片
本身的轉換效率提升。 
表一. Technical Performance Benchmarks 
資料來源：ASE、國泰證期研究部整理

產業報告

5 
VPD 的產業成熟度亦正由封裝平台驗證逐步向實際 AI 晶片應用推進。Meta 於 APEC 2026 發表
850A AI ASIC 垂直供電模組，輸出電壓為 0.85V，採兩級供電架構，電流密度達 0.8A/mm²，並
已完成設計、製造及電性與散熱驗證。隨 AI ASIC 及 GPU 所需電流持續提高，研究部預期 VPD
後續競爭重點將由單純電源轉換效率，進一步延伸至單位面積電流密度、封裝高度、散熱能力、
瞬時響應速度及封裝面積。 
先進封裝由訊號互連走向供電整合，OSAT 價值量持續提升 
SEMICON T aiwan 2026所反映的重要產業變化，在於 VPD 已由單一封裝技術逐步轉為 AI 先進
封裝的核心設計參數。日月光投控於本屆論壇將 VPD 與超過 1,000A 的 AI 處理器、HBM 配置
及封裝內電壓調節整合放在同一技術架構下 ， 顯示先進封裝的功能正由過去以RDL、TSV、Chiplet
及 HBM 高速互連為主，進一步延伸至供電、電源完整性及散熱管理。 
隨電壓調節模組逐步靠近 XPU，封裝內被動元件的重要性亦同步提高。高功耗 XPU 在運算負載
快速切換時，需要鄰近的去耦電容即時提供瞬時電流，因此供電元件向運算晶片靠近的同時，電
容及其他被動元件亦需同步向封裝內部整合。台積電 CoWoS-L 已可於 SoC 下方整合 eDTC 以
改善電源完整性；日月光本次 SEMICON 亦同步展示 CAP Module 及 FOCoS-Bridge 等技術，
反映 AI 先進封裝正由單純訊號整合，逐步轉向訊號、供電與被動元件共同整合。 
未來 AI 封裝中的供電相關內容 ， 預期將不再侷限於電壓調節模組 ， 而可進一步涵蓋電源級晶片 、
矽電容、eDTC、MIM 電容、整合式磁性元件及嵌入式被動元件。隨 AI XPU 功耗提高，供電效
率、電源元件配置及散熱架構將逐步成為高階封裝的重要差異化來源，單顆 AI 封裝的價值量亦
有望由原有晶粒組裝與高速互連持續向供電整合延伸。 
圖三：ASE PowerSiP

資料來源：ASE、國泰證期研究部整理 
研究部認為，VPD 對 OSAT 產業的結構性意義，在於先進封裝開始承擔部分過去由主機板負責
的供電功能。若 VPD 後續於 AI GPU 及客製化 ASIC 的滲透率提高，OSAT 的角色將由傳統封裝
測試及互連服務，進一步升級為涵蓋運算、記憶體、供電及散熱的系統整合平台，並帶動單顆高
階 AI 封裝的供電相關價值量持續提升。

### Page 6

投資 顧問 
產業報告

6 
4Q26 晶背供電與垂直供電帶動晶片架構升級 
研究部認為 ，AI 半導體下一階段的技術瓶頸將由運算效能與記憶體頻寬進一步延伸至供電架構 。
隨 AI XPU 功耗、封裝尺寸及供電電流同步提高，傳統由晶圓正面及主機板進行長距離供電的架
構，逐步面臨壓降、傳輸損耗及布線空間不足等限制，促使產業分別由晶片與封裝兩端縮短供電
路徑。晶圓端以 BSPDN 將電源線路由晶圓正面移至背面，降低供電阻抗並釋放正面訊號布線空
間；封裝端則透過 VPD 將電壓調節模組由 XPU 側邊移至下方，縮短低壓大電流的傳輸距離。隨
兩項技術逐步落地，Power Delivery 將由過去的電源零組件問題，進一步成為先進製程及先進封
裝共同面對的半導體架構議題。 
晶圓代工端最明確的 4Q26 催化劑為台積電(2330 TT)A16 進入量產。A16 將奈米片 GAA 與
Super Power Rail(SPR)晶背供電整合，代表 BSPDN 正式由技術開發階段進入商業製造。相較
傳統先進製程僅持續增加前段製程複雜度，晶背供電在正面製程完成後，還需額外進行晶圓接
合、極薄化、晶背蝕刻、平坦化、介電層與金屬層形成，以及後續檢測，因此新增的是一整段
晶背加工流程。隨 A16 於 4Q26 量產，先進製程設備需求的成長來源將不僅來自 2 奈米以下晶
圓投片量提高，單片晶圓所需的製程步驟及耗材使用量亦將同步增加，其中化學機械平坦化
(CMP)、晶圓薄化、蝕刻、薄膜沉積、晶圓接合及檢測量測均為主要受惠環節。 
台股供應鏈中，中砂(1560 TT)與昇陽半導體(8028 TT）可望受惠 BSPDN 導入。中砂主要受惠
晶背薄化與平坦化需求增加，BSPDN 對背面平坦度、厚度均勻性與表面品質要求提高，將推升
CMP 製程與耗材使用量；中砂透過 PYRADIA 系列鑽石碟切入先進 CMP 市場，受惠來源除先
進製程投片成長外，亦包括單片晶圓 CMP 製程步驟增加。昇陽半導體則受惠晶背製程增加所帶
動的測試與監控晶圓需求。隨晶背薄化、研磨、蝕刻、沉積與平坦化等製程增加，設備調機、
製程驗證及量產監控所需再生晶圓同步提升。憑藉再生晶圓加工與精密拋光能力，昇陽半導體
有望受惠先進製程複雜度提高所帶來的再生晶圓需求成長。 
4Q26 VPD 的產業重點將由技術可行性驗證逐步轉向客戶導入與前量產準備。ASE powerSiP 雖
已於 24 年推出，但 SEMICON T aiwan 2026顯示，隨下一代 AI 處理器供電需求朝 1,000A 以上
發展，VPD 已由單一電源封裝方案升級為 AI 先進封裝的重要架構選項；Meta 於 APEC 2026 完
成 850A AI ASIC 垂直供電模組的設計、製造及電性與散熱驗證，也顯示大型雲端業者已開始將
VPD 納入實際 AI ASIC 供電設計。因此 4Q26 是 VPD 由平台開發進入客戶驗證、設計導入及量
產準備的重要階段，而非大規模出貨起點。對OSAT 而言，隨 VPD 將電壓調節模組由 XPU 側邊
移至下方，先進封裝的價值來源亦將由過去以RDL、TSV、Chiplet 及 HBM 高速互連為主，逐步
延伸至供電與電源完整性；若 VPD 於 AI GPU 及客製化 ASIC 的滲透率提高，封裝內新增內容可
望進一步涵蓋電壓調節模組、矽電容、eDTC、嵌入式被動元件及散熱結構，使日月光投控(3711 
TT)等 OSAT 業者由傳統封裝測試與互連服務，逐步轉向運算、記憶體、供電與散熱的系統整合
平台，並帶動單顆高階 AI 封裝價值量持續提升。

《貨幣政策-長債殖利率攀升，聯準會極欲證明自身
對通膨的決心》
美國8月公布的就業 (非農月減2.3萬人)、通膨(核心
CPI年增2.5%)、消費數據(零售銷售月減 0.6%)，均
指向聯準會無需急於升息，9月升息的機率一度降到
40%以下，帶動美國短天期公債殖利率下滑 ，然長
天期利率卻持續上揚。
8月底聯準會主席華許在 Jackson Hole發表演說 ，
他表示「目前的數據並沒有讓我相信 ，通膨趨勢已
經出現有意義的改善」、「2%是一個堅定；固定的
通膨目標」、「目前的利率與金融條件並不具有限
制性」。鷹派的發言，使9月升息的機率再度攀升至
50%左右。
9月是否升息 ，關鍵在於 9月時公布的 8月通膨數據 。
本行仍認為9月按兵不動的機率略高於升息，主因：

(1)綜合市場預估和先前趨勢推演 ，本行認為 8月核
心CPI月增率將落在 0.2%左右。若此，聯準會有理
由採取觀望。
(2)美國近期失業率呈現下行趨勢 ，但主要是因勞動
參與率同步下滑所致 ，新增就業人數其實相當溫和 ，
今年以來沒有任何一個月份新增達到 20萬人，7月
甚至月減2.3萬人。。
風險：近一年來通膨的壓力大多集中在能源和商品
類別，服務通膨表現溫和，若8月看到服務通膨數據
出現上行壓力 ，則聯準會將不得不採取行動已證明
其公信力。
重點摘要
• 長債殖利率攀升 ，聯準會極
欲證明自身對通膨的決心

《美股盤勢-獲利成長和利率壓力拉鋸，美股短線有
壓然無礙趨勢》
歷史經驗顯示 ，無風險利率上揚時期 ，股市情緒通
常受壓抑 ，然今年以來美 10年期利率自 4%上升至
4.7%，不時有對短線盤勢造成干擾 ，但並未明顯影
響趨勢，主要是因近年的特殊環境：在 AI的強力趨
勢面前，即使資金成本很高 ，大企業仍持續擴大投
資，且確實帶來獲利 ，獲利的成長大過估值的壓縮
幅度。標普500指數在2Q26的企業獲利年增率預估
將來到50%，雖然這是因大型科技公司將其私募投
資持股市價重估後產生的收益 ，但排除後，獲利年
增率仍達30%。另一方面，因為資本市場活絡 ，金
融流動性大多時間維持在寬鬆區間 ，並沒有因利率
上揚或聯準會的鷹派預期而進入緊縮。

因此，本行仍維持美股的正向看法 ，不過短線震盪
風險仍是加大 ，在財報季告一段落後 ，市場仍很可
能開始放大檢視利率上揚帶來的影響 ，尤其在市場
開始討論AI生態系投資與算力融資之際 。研判市場
資金將維持輪動格局 ，獲利擴散的順週期類股如金
融、工業，估值具有優勢的防禦類股如健護 ，將可
望受惠；科技內部也開始輪動 ，資金不再只高度集
中在AI硬體。
重點摘要
• 本行仍維持美股的正向看法 ，
不過短線震盪風險仍是加大 ，

《拉美股市-低估值搭配改革紅利，巴西選舉是關鍵
變數》
目前市場共識看多拉美股市的核心支柱是政策轉型 。
拉美許多國家正轉向放鬆監管 、提升企業投資誘因 、
強調財政紀律 ，而巴西大選將是重要的觀察點 。巴
西目前的總統魯拉的立場較為偏左 ，且不親美 ，他
的對手波索納洛會是較符合市場對於提升企業投資
誘因期待的選擇 。另外，在全球AI與資料中心快速
發展的關鍵時期 ，巴西有望在能源 、礦產、基礎建
設與數位經濟供應鏈中扮演更重要角色 ，加上巴西
擁有全球第二的稀土蘊藏量 ，目前因為煉製技術落
後因此開採能量有限 ，但在西方國家不願受制於中
國的環境下 ，若政權轉換 ，預計將會有更多的礦業
投資計畫在巴西展開。
新興市場的資金在2Q26主要往AI供應鏈走，拉美並

不受青睞 ，然近期資金已開始尋找 AI供應鏈外的價
值投資機會 ， 從估值角度來看 ， 拉美股市的
forward PE相對其他市場明顯低了不少 ，本行仍維
持拉美股市的正向看法。
不過估值要有明顯的擴張 ，需要政策方向更加可信 ，
財政更有紀律 ，讓過高的利率有辦法降下來 ，同樣
和巴西年底選舉後的政策走向息息相關。
重點摘要
• 看多拉美股市的核心支柱在
於政策轉型 。拉美許多國家
正轉向放鬆監管 、提升企業
投資誘因、強調財政紀律
• 拉美股市的 forward PE相對
其他市場明顯低了不少 ，低
估值本身提供了一定程度的
安全邊際 ，不過估值要有明
顯的擴張 ，需要政策方向更
加可信 ，財政更有紀律 ，讓
過高的利率有辦法降下來
5
資料來源：摩根士丹利，2026/8，中國信託商業銀行彙整， 2026/8
巴西股市估值仍處偏低水位

Source PDF: all report pdf/イベントカレンダー『各国の主要政治・経済イベント予定』(2026年10月号).pdf

(欧)7-9月期GDP速報 （30日)
（注） 作成時点で、 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所経済調査部が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、 その正確性、 完全性に対す

る責任は負いません。
日銀短観 （全国企業短期経済観測調査） は、 日本銀行が全国の企業約1万社を対象に年4回実施する代表的な経
済統計です。 企業の景況感を示す 「業況判断DI （良いと答えた割合から悪いと答えた割合を引いた指数） 」 を中心に、
企業の事業計画や物価見通しをいち早く捉えることができるため、 日銀の金融政策を左右する重要なデータと位置
付けられています。
直近 ６ 月の業況判断DIを見ると、 原油高や資材価格上昇の影響が懸念されたものの、 大企業 ・ 製造業はＡ Ｉ ・ 半導体
関連や設備投資関連の需要が強く、 景況感が改善しました （資料） 。 また、 大企業 ・ 非製造業もインバウン ド需要や底
堅い個人消費が下支えとなり高水準を維持しています。 日本経済の裾野を支える中小企業についても、 人手不足や
コス ト高の負担から大企業と比べると水準はやや低いものの、 好況を
維持しており、 足もとで目立った悪化は見られません。
こう した中で10月1日に公表される 「9月短観」 は、 日銀が年内の追

加利上げに踏み切るかどうかの重要な試金石となります。 最大の焦点
は、 「賃金と物価の好循環」 が維持されているかどうかです。 具体的に
は、 企業がコス ト増を販売価格へ転嫁できているか、 旺盛な設備投資
意欲が続いているかが注目され、 これらが堅調なら追加利上げの判断
を後押しすることになります。 逆に景況感が大きく下振れすれば、 利上
げペースへの慎重論が台頭する可能性があるため、 市場の注目が高
まっています。 （主席エコノミス ト ：  阿原健一郎）

时可全部归属； 2027 年和 2028 年的初始交付额预计为 24 亿美元。
AI 算⼒链条的其他环节也同步⾛强。超微电脑涨 9.50% ，⾼盛预计到 2030 年全球 AI 服务器市场
规模将达到 1.3 万亿美元；甲⻣⽂涨 5.18% 。

形成反差，令不少市场⼈⼠对后市跟进能⼒持保留态度。

味着若指数跌破该⽔平，价格波动可能被放⼤。
此外，商品交易顾问（ CTA ）模型显⽰，未来⼀周内或将出现约 300 亿美元的美股抛售。
从历史规律来看，美联储加息周期的最终幅度往往远超市场在加息初期的预期。

### Page 5

德意志银⾏数据显⽰，⾃ 1950 年代以来的 15 次加息周期，平均持续 22 个⽉，平均累计加息幅度
达 478 个基点。⽽⽬前市场定价的本轮加息周期总幅度仅约 95 个基点，若最终如此，将成为现代
史上最浅的⼀次加息周期。
九⽉下旬历来是美股季节性偏弱的时间窗⼝，也是 VIX 倾向于⾛⾼的阶段 —— 这令此次反弹的持
久性仍有待检验。
债市为沃什的抗通胀公信⼒定价， 10 年期美债回落⾄ 4.95%
美国国债收益率在周四⾛低，部分源于住房数据⾛弱，同时英国国债的⼤幅反弹也形成共振。
10 年期收益率跌 5.8 个基点⾄ 4.95% ，为三个多星期以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，也终结了连续⼋个交易
⽇的上涨，此前⼀天刚触及 19 年⾼位。
2 年期跌 3.8 个基点⾄ 4.688% ，30 年期跌 5.0 个基点⾄ 5.296% 。路透援引交易⼈⼠称，此前押注
收益率上⾏的对冲基⾦正在获利了结。
花旗分析师 Michael Chang 表⽰，美联储在对抗通胀尾部⻛险上看起来是可信的，这对债券投资
者是利好，⻓期通胀预期在加息当天和次⽇都在回落。

Mischler Financial 董事总经理 Tom di Galoma 说，没⼈愿意再做空⻓端了，财政部的回购计划也
在帮助抬升⻓债需求。 LNW ⾸席投资官 Ron Albahary 则认为，市场先是看到利率上⾏，现在回
吐了⼀部分，但还没有真正搞明⽩发⽣了什么。
英国央⾏当⽇以 6 票对 3 票维持基准利率在 3.75% 不变，三名委员主张加息 25 个基点。更关键的
是，央⾏宣布取消⻓期国债的出售计划，全部量化紧缩拍卖暂停⾄明年 4 ⽉。
规模 4880 亿英镑的资产组合改为在 2034 年前通过到期与定向出售两种⽅式缩减。英国 30 年期国
债收益率单⽇下跌 11 个基点，为 5 ⽉ 20 ⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，10 年期跌⾄ 5.295% 。

### Page 6

⾏⻓⻉利在致财政⼤⾂的信中表⽰，这⼀安排维护了货币政策的独⽴性。瑞银指出，多位投票
维持利率不变的委员表⽰，若冲突与能源冲击持续，加息的理由正在积累。
油价是关键变量，但供应⻛险未除
周四原油价格下跌，是推动市场情绪转暖的重要催化剂。
WTI 原油10 ⽉合约结算价跌 0.52 美元⾄ 101.91 美元 / 桶，跌幅 0.51% ，盘中⼀度跌破 100 美元。
布伦特原油11 ⽉合约结算价跌 1.01 美元⾄ 104.82 美元 / 桶，跌幅 0.95% ，盘中最⼤跌幅⼀度超过
3% ，触及 9 ⽉ 10 ⽇以来最低⽔平。进⼊美国交易时段后，油价⾃低位回补。
产品价格涨跌互现，汽油价格创下周期新⾼，⽽柴油价格暴跌，取暖油价格也出现下滑。

### Page 7

据报道，沙特阿拉伯计划在数⽇内恢复其关键东西向管道约⼀半的运输能⼒，同时已向亚洲炼
油商出售更多原油，交货地点设在霍尔⽊兹海峡以外。
然⽽， CIBC 私⼈财富集团⾼级能源交易员 Rebecca Babin 提醒，供应整体仍偏紧，⽀撑了美股
交易时段内油价的部分回升。 Global Risk Management ⾸席分析师 Arne Lohmann Rasmussen
则表⽰：
摩根⼤通分析师 Natasha Kaneva 在报告中指出，美伊冲突对能源流通的扰动 " 越来越难以预
测 " ， " 这是⾃冲突开始以来，我们第⼀次没有基准预测，我们根本不知道如何对最终结局建
模。 "
贵⾦属是当天涨幅第⼆⼤的资产类别。现货⻩⾦涨 2.3% ⾄ 4360.36 美元 / 盎司，从前⼀交易⽇的
近六周低位反弹，盘中最⾼触及 4381.07 美元。纽约商品交易所 12 ⽉交割的⻩⾦期货结算价上涨
0.3% ⾄ 4399.70 美元 / 盎司。
现货⽩银涨 3.83% ⾄ 65.66 美元 / 盎司，盘中⼀度涨 5% ⾄ 66.13 美元。

High Ridge Futures ⾦属交易主管 David Meger 表⽰，⻩⾦近期与能源价格保持着紧密的反向关
系，能源价格当天⼤幅回落，正是这些更低的能源价格卸下了压在⻩⾦市场⾝上的⼀部分压
我们将此视为原油和成品油的逢低买⼊机会，价格虽有所回落，但潜在的供应⻛险并未消失。

Document 546: 在職老齢年金の見直しでシニア就労は増えたか－65～69歳男性の制度改正直後の変化

Source PDF: all report pdf/在職老齢年金の見直しでシニア就労は増えたか－65～69歳男性の制度改正直後の変化.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

1――はじめに

老齢厚生年金を受給しながら企業などで働き、年金（報酬比例部分）と賃金1の合計額が一定金額を
超えると、年金が減額される「在職老齢年金制度」は、高齢者の働く意欲を低下させると考えられて
いる。少子高齢化によって現役世代の負担が重くなる中で、報酬のある高齢者は「支える側」に回っ
てもらおうという考え方に基づく仕組みだが、企業側の人材確保や技能承継、または厚生年金適用事
業所以外で働く高齢者との公平性などの観点から、見直しを求める意見が多く、2026年 4月から、年
金が減額される基準額2が「月51万円」から「月65万円」に引き上げられた。しかし、いざ基準額を
引き上げれば、実際に高齢者の労働供給が増えるかどうかは、これまでの実証研究でも、必ずしも明
白になっていない。そこで本稿では、引き上げ後の2026年 4 月から 6 月までの四半期の高齢者の就
業状況を調べ、引き上げ前と比べて変化が見られたかどうかを検証する。

2――在職老齢年金による就業調整の実態

在職老齢年金制度が、高齢者の働く意欲を低下させるという根拠としてよく用いられるのが、内閣
府が 2023年に全国の 18 歳以上の男女を対象に行った「生活設計と年金に関する世論調査」である。
在職老齢年金制度の仕組みを説明した上で、公的年金を受け取る年齢になった時の働き方を尋ねたと
ころ、「働かない」が男性では21.0%、女性では26.0%、「年金額が減らないように、就業時間を調整
しながら会社などで働く」が男性は41.9%、女性は46.6%だった（図表1）。ただし、この調査は対象
に現役世代も含めて将来の見込みを聞いたものであり、支給開始年齢に至った時に、実際にそのよう
な行動をとるかどうかは定かではない。 
 これに対して、総務省の「令和 4年就業構造基本調査」は調査時点の有業者に対し、実際に就業調

1 その月の標準報酬月額に、1 年間の標準賞与額を 12 で割った額を足した金額。 
2 支給停止調整額。

3――在職老齢年金によって就業調整する男性は約 10 万人と試算

次に、現在、在職老齢年金を意識して就業調整している男性の人数を試算する。上述したように、
「令和４年就業構造基本調査」によると、非正規雇用で就業調整している男性は約100 万人である。
JILPTの調査によると、就業調整している短時間労働の男性のうち、在職老齢年金を理由とする人は
約１割であるため、先ほどの100万人に 1割を乗じて、本稿では、在職老齢年金制度によって就業調
整する男性は現在、約10万人いると試算する。なお、在職老齢年金制度の影響としては、就業時間を
調整しながら働く人だけではなく、そもそも「働かない」という選択をしている人もいると考えられ
るので、制度によって就業抑制している人数は、これより多いと考えられる。

4――制度見直しでシニア就労は増えるのか――先行研究から

ここまで、在職老齢年金制度が高齢男性の就業抑制につながっていることを見てきたが、それでは、
減額基準額を引き上げると、実際に高齢者の労働供給は増えるのだろうか。実は、先行研究でもこの
あたりは必ずしも明白になっていない。 
 OSHIO et al. (2019) は、厚生労働省「中高年齢者縦断調査」のパネルデータを用いて、国内の高齢
者の就業継続に関する意思決定と、年金制度との関係を検討した5。その制度のうちの一つが、在職老
齢年金制度廃止である。分析では、高齢者が、仕事からの引退を1年先延ばしにすると、生涯にわた
って受け取る年金総額や、追加的に支払う税・社会保険料の合計がどうなるかを、個人ごとに算出す
る変数を作成した。そして、各個人の就業（パートまたはフルタイム）や引退という選択と、労働時
間の意思決定に関して、回帰分析を行った。 
 その結果、 65～69 歳男性に関しては、在職老齢年金制度を廃止した場合は、「引退」という選択は
-0.7ポイント、「フルタイム就業」の選択は+1.0ポイント、「パートタイム就業」の選択は-0.3ポイン

5 OSHIO et al. (2019) “Addressing the Institutional Disincentives to Elderly Employment in Japan”, RIETI Discussion 
Paper Series 19-E-080.

5――2026 年 4 月の制度見直し後、シニア就労は変わったか

5-1 | 就業率に変化はあったか 
 次に、 2026 年 4 月に在職老齢年金制度で年金が減額となる基準額が引き上げられた後、高齢男性
の労働供給に変化があったかどうかを見るため、総務省「労働力調査」より、65～69 歳男性と 70～
74歳男性について、過去5年間の 4～6月期の就業率と対前年同期比の推移を示した（図表3、4）。 
2026年 4～6 月期の 65～69歳男性の就業率は65.2%で、前年同期より2.8ポイント増加した。過
去 5 年間の対前年同期比の推移を見ると、2022 年以降、プラスマイナス2 ポイント以内で小幅な増
減を繰り返しつつも、緩やかに上昇傾向であったが、2026年は上昇幅が2ポイントを超え、上昇幅が

6――終わりに

本稿では、2026年 4 月に在職老齢年金制度の減額基準額が月51 万円から 65 万円へ引き上げられ
たことを受けて、その直後の4～6月期に、高齢男性の就業状況に変化が見られたかを検討した。在職
老齢年金制度については、年金の減額を避けるために高齢者が就業時間や収入を抑える「壁」として、
かねて就業抑制効果が指摘されてきた。実際、本稿で既存調査を基に試算したところ、在職老齢年金
制度を意識して就業調整している男性は約10万人と試算された。 その意味では、基準額の引き上げ
は、高齢者がより自由に働ける環境を整える上で重要な制度改正だと言える。 
 しかし、制度を見直せば、直ちに高齢者の労働供給が大きく増えるとは限らない。今回の分析では、
65～69歳男性の就業率は前年同期から2.8ポイント上昇し、近年に比べて上昇幅がわずかに拡大した
一方、70～74 歳男性では従来の上昇傾向から大きな変化は見られなかった。 また、就業時間につい
ては、65～69歳、70～74歳のいずれも明確な増加は確認できなかった。制度見直しから3か月とい
う短期間のデータではあるが、現時点では、基準額の引き上げによって高齢男性の労働供給が明確に
増加した、とまでは言えない。 
 本稿の分析結果は、在職老齢年金制度が高齢者の就業を左右する数ある要因の一つに過ぎないこと
を示唆している。特に 60 代後半以降になると、働くかどうか、どの程度働くかは、収入だけではな
く、健康や体力、仕事の内容、勤務時間、家庭の状況など、様々な条件によって決まる。 したがって、
年金の減額基準額を引き上げることは、働きたい高齢者の就業を妨げる制度上の制約を緩和する効果

7 坊美生子(2026)「上昇に陰りが見え始めたシニアの就業率－60 代前半男性は頭打ち、今後の注目はシニア女性に」（基礎
研レポート） 
8 同(2026)「定年後の『ちょうどいい』働き方とは～拙著『女性たちの定年後～お金・仕事・暮らしのリアル』（祥伝社新
書）より(4)」（研究員の眼）

Report source: DLRI

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年９月 14 日

1/3 
大接戦のスウェーデン総選挙 
～スウェーデン民主党は失速、政権交代に傾く～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ ９月 13 日のスウェーデン総選挙は、開票速報で社会民主党を中心とする現野党陣営が現与
党陣営を僅差でリードする。予想獲得議席は175 対 174 の１議席差で、在外投票などを含む
最終結果まで勝敗が確定しない可能性がある。 
○ 勝敗の鍵とみられた自由党は議席獲得に必要な４％を突破した一方、前回選挙で右派最大党
に躍進したスウェーデン民主党は支持を落とし、右派陣営内で二番手に後退した模様だ。 
○ 現野党陣営の勝利が確定すれば、アンデション前首相率いる社会民主党主導の政権発足が有
力となる。ただ、中央党と左翼党の政策的な隔たりは大きく、幅広い連立が難航すれば、社
会民主党を中心とする非多数派政権となる可能性がある。 
○ 政権交代の場合、福祉・再分配・気候政策の修正が予想される一方、移民・治安政策の厳格
化や NATO 加盟、防衛力強化といった過去４年間の政策転換が大幅に巻き戻される可能性は
低い。最終結果で右派が逆転すれば、スウェーデン民主党の初入閣が焦点となる。

９月 13 日に投開票が行われたスウェーデンの総選挙は、日本時間14 日９時時点の開票速報
で、アンデション前首相が率いる社会民主主義系・中道左派の「社会民主党（Ｓ） 」を中心とする
野党陣営が、クリステンション首相が率いる保守系・中道右派の「穏健党（Ｍ） 」や現政権に閣外
協力するナショナリスト系・右派ポピュリスト政党の「スウェーデン民主党（ＳＤ） 」を中心とす
る与党陣営を僅かにリードする（図表１） 。開票速報時点の両陣営の得票率は現野党の 49.4％と現
与党の49.1％とその差は僅か、予想獲得議席は175 対174 と大接戦で、政権交代の成否が確定す
るのは、在外投票や期日前投票の結果が加わる週末の最終結果までずれ込む可能性がある。 
なお、定数349 のうち310 議席（固定議席）は、有権者数などに応じて29 の選挙区に予め割り
当てられ、各選挙区内の得票率に比例して各党に配分される。残りの39 議席（調整議席）は、固
定議席の占有率が全国の得票率を下回った政党に配分される。例えば、全国で20％の得票率を獲
得した政党が、選挙区の固定議席で配分された議席の占有率が18％にとどまった場合、20％に近
づくように調整議席の一部がこの党に配分される。調整議席の配分も最終的な結果を左右する。

### Page 2

2/3

８月 28 日付けレポート「揺れるスウェーデン政局～総選挙で問う右旋回の４年～」で指摘した
通り、両陣営は事前の世論調査でも接戦を繰り広げ、現与党のリベラル系・中道右派の「自由党
（Ｌ）」が議席獲得に必要な４％に届くかが、勝敗の鍵を握るとみられていた。選挙戦の最終盤で
自由党の支持率が急速に巻き返し、最終的に４％を突破したが、スウェーデン民主党が直前の世
論調査を下回る得票率にとどまり、現与党陣営は結局、現野党陣営の逆転を許した模様だ。結党
以来、総選挙の度に支持を上積みしてきたスウェーデン民主党の得票率は前回総選挙を下回り、
現与党陣営内で二番手に転落した（図表２） 。スウェーデン民主党の支持率が伸び悩んだのは、①
極右政党と称されることもあるスウェーデン民主党が次期政権を主導することを警戒し、現与党
支持者の一部が穏健党などに流れたほか、現野党支持者の投票率が高まった、②自由党の議席獲
得と右派陣営の勝利を目指し、スウェーデン民主党支持者の一部が自由党に戦略的に投票した―
可能性がある。スウェーデン民主党は前回総選挙後、右派陣営内の最大政党となりながら、一部
政党からの反対もあり、連立政権への正式参加を見送った。現与党陣営が勝利する場合、今回は
連立政権に参加する意向を示唆しており、陣営内もそれを容認する方向だ。

りません。 
3/3

このまま開票速報通り、現野党陣営の勝利が確定した場合、最大政党となる社会民主党が主導
する連立政権が誕生し、アンデション氏が首相に復帰する可能性がある。ただ、現野党の左派政
党の「左翼党（Ｖ） 」 、環境政党の「緑の党（ＭＰ） 」 、リベラル系・中道政党の「中央党（Ｃ） 」の
間には、経済運営などを巡って意見対立もある。特に福祉拡大や富裕層増税などを主張する左翼
党の政権参加に中央党は否定的だ。首相就任・政権発足には議会の投票が必要で、定数349 の過
半数に相当する175 人以上が反対すると否決され、反対票が174 票以下なら承認される（175 人以
上の賛成は必要ない） 。連立協議が暗礁に乗り上げた場合も、現野党陣営の一部が現与党陣営の政
権誕生に賛成票や棄権票を投じる可能性は低く、社会民主党が主導する非多数派政権が誕生する
ことが予想される。社会民主党を中心とした左派政権が誕生する場合、福祉・再分配・気候政策
が修正される可能性が高い一方、現政権下で厳格化した移民政策や治安対策の大幅な巻き戻し、
NATO加盟や防衛力強化の方針撤回は考えにくい。 
最終的な投票結果で右派が逆転した場合、スウェーデン民主党が連立政権に加わる可能性が高
い。今回は右派陣営内で二番手に転落したとみられ、連立政権を主導する可能性は遠退いたが、
結党以来で初めて閣僚ポストを手にすることになる。政権内での影響力が一段と拡大するかどう
かが注目を集めよう。 
以 上

### Page 1

David Einhorn 认为美国财政失控、美元储备地位受侵蚀及去美元化趋势持续强化⻩⾦⻓期
价值，⻩⾦是绿光资本最⼤宏观多头仓位。与此同时，他警告当前主导纳指的⼤型科技巨
头，正从轻资产垄断模式转向资本密集型竞争业务， AI 资本⽀出带来的折旧压⼒将逐步侵
蚀盈利。
摘要：
绿光资本创始⼈ David Einhorn 预计，未来三到五年，⻩⾦将⼤幅跑赢纳斯达克，且差距可能相
当悬殊。
（左：绿光资本创始⼈ David Einhorn ，右：摩根⼠丹利 Iliana Bouzali ）
在近期接受摩根⼠丹利采访时， David Einhorn 发出警告，纳斯达克⼤型科技股正⾯临从 “ 轻资产
垄断 ”商业模式向资本密集型竞争业务的根本性转变，⽽⻩⾦的⻓期上⾏逻辑持续强化。
Einhorn 将这⼀论断定性为 “ ⼤胆预测 ” ，他表⽰，美国财政状况持续失控，美元储备地位受到侵
蚀，叠加全球 " 去美元化 " 趋势，⻩⾦的⻓期战略价值正在系统性提升。
与此同时，他认为 AI 资本⽀出浪潮正在为科技巨头的利润埋下⻓期隐患。

这⼀判断直接体现于绿光资本的持仓结构。 Einhorn 透露，⻩⾦⽬前是该基⾦最⼤的宏观多头头
⼨，且⾃ 2008 年起便⻓期持有。他强调：
⻩⾦：财政失控下的⾸选避险资产
Einhorn 对⻩⾦的看涨逻辑，根植于对全球财政和货币秩序的⻓期担忧。
他认为，主要经济体尤其是美国的财政⾚字问题远未得到控制。他直接点名美国财政部⻓的 3%
⾚字占 GDP ⽬标，表⽰：
只要联邦预算不受控制，⻩⾦就是我的朋友。
我不知道他要如何实现这⼀⽬标，⽬前看来进展甚微。

### Page 2

在货币体系层⾯， Einhorn 指出，西⽅国家冻结俄罗斯外汇储备⼀事是⼀个标志性信号，令众多
国家重新评估持有美元资产的安全性。他表⽰：
他补充称，此轮⻩⾦今年 1 ⽉⾄ 2 ⽉出现的抛物线式急涨之后，短期可能进⼊消化期， Einhorn 指
出：
他强调，这是⼀个多年期的⻓期判断，五年内⻩⾦应会消化投机性峰值并恢复上⾏。
科技巨头：从 " 资本轻型 " 到 " 资本密集型 " 的痛苦蜕变
Einhorn 对纳斯达克的悲观预期，核⼼在于对 AI 资本⽀出热潮的冷静解剖。
他认为，当前科技板块的利润存在严重的 " 虚胖 " 成分，折旧滞后效应将在未来逐步侵蚀盈利。
他以内存芯⽚为例说明这⼀机制：若内存价格涨⾄原来的五倍，内存供应商利润爆发，但超⼤
规模云计算商所⽀出的⾼价设备被资本化处理，当期费⽤为零，折旧压⼒递延⾄未来。
Einhorn 表⽰：
这意味着，届时企业盈利将完全依赖 AI 业务的增量回报，⽽这⼀回报⾯临极⾼的竞争压⼒。
更根本的判断是，⼤型科技公司正在丧失维系⾼估值的商业模式基础。 Einhorn 强调：

AI 价值链：受益者是⽤⼾，⽽⾮提供商
对于 AI 的⻓期产业格局， Einhorn 持有⼀个与市场主流叙事相悖的观点：AI 创造的价值，最终将
主要流向⽤⼾⽽⾮提供商。
他指出， AI 领域缺乏构建⻓期垄断利润所必需的 " 护城河 " 。与 Facebook 等社交平台的⽹络效应
不同， AI 的使⽤体验⾼度个⼈化，⽤⼾之间并不形成相互强化的⽹络价值。
与传统软件的规模经济不同， AI 是资本密集型业务，边际成本并不趋近于零。 Einhorn 指出：
储备的意义在于需要时能动⽤，如果做不到，那还叫什么储备？
即便⻩⾦⼀年甚⾄⼀年半内毫⽆动静，我也不会感到意外。
我看到⼀项测算显⽰，若按⽬前的 AI 建设规模推算，到 2033 年，折旧费⽤将与超⼤规模云计算
商现有业务的利润持平，⽽这还是假设现有业务继续按历史速度增⻓的前提下。
它们正在从轻资产垄断型业务，转向资本密集型的竞争激烈业务。在资本密集型⾏业，资本追逐
回报，超额回报最终会被竞争消耗殆尽。⼀旦发⽣，纳斯达克估值就将⾯临重估。
如果有 10 家 AI 提供商都能提供⼤致相同的服务，⽤⼾可以低成本随时切换，那提供商根本⽆法

 債市展望：美國財政部雙管齊下阻止長天期美債殖利率

日本央行10年綜合通膨預期指數已接近2% 圖表解讀

號。按照過去數次會議日本央行的立場判斷 ，9月會議
可能再次宣布升息25bps至1.25%。
➢ 德國8月通膨年率預期由 7月的2.8%走揚至2.9%，而歐
元區8月通膨年率也預期由 7月的2.9%上升至3.1%，使
市場進一步押注9月ECB會議升息機率。除近期高漲的能
源價格外，擴張性財政政策 、綠色轉型、國防支出增加
及勞動市場都是歐洲通膨重新升溫的原因。
➢ 而美國數據看點在於目前市場景氣 (PMI)能否延續強勢 ，
而7月意外下滑的就業數據是否出現扭轉，若PMI續強且
非農再次轉向正成長，市場或再次提升未來升息押注。

### Page 8

中東擔憂消退，原油回吐近月漲幅
8
匯市表現說明
➢ WTI原油：WTI原油回吐近月漲幅，反映市場對中東衝突
擴散至主要產油國或關鍵航道的擔憂有所降溫 ，地緣政
治風險溢價逐步消退 。儘管美元走弱通常有利原油價格 ，
但市場焦點已轉向全球需求與供需平衡 。油價維持區間
震盪有助控制通膨壓力 ，對全球市場是相對有利的發展 。
➢ 澳幣： 澳幣表現居主要已開發國家前段班 ，作為典型的
商品貨幣 ，受惠市場風險偏好回升及亞洲經濟活動改善
帶動原物料需求增加 。隨著美元走弱及資金重新流向高
收益市場 ，澳洲相對較高的利率水準亦提升澳幣吸引力 ，
反映投資人對全球經濟前景仍維持樂觀態度。
➢ 英鎊： 近期英國薪資增速與服務業通膨仍具韌性 ，使市
場重新評估英國央行政策路徑 ，帶動英國公債殖利率維
持相對高位 。然而，市場對英國經濟較歐元區信心不足 ，
即便年內啟動升息 ，也預測於明年將重啟降息 ，多數則
預期利率將維持不動，使英鎊短期表現略遜一籌。

### Page 9

美元為何轉弱？問題在於利率結構變化
9
2年利率下滑且期限溢價上揚，美元表現偏弱 圖表解讀
➢ 歷史數據顯示 ，當市場預期聯準會短期將不會升息或
甚至降息時，2年期殖利率通常會下滑 ，且投資人因財
政赤字與發債增加而要求更高期限溢價時 ，美元往往
表現較弱 。此現象下 ，長天期殖利率上升並非來自經
濟轉強，而是風險補償需求增加。
➢ 當美國短端利率優勢逐漸縮小時 ，全球資金配置方向
將發生改變 ，使美元指數回落 ，而黃金因實質利率壓
力減輕而重新具有吸引力 。比特幣則受惠於流動性改
善與風險偏好回升 。近期在美國財政部宣布回購長債
後，資金便加速流向黃金及比特幣等替代標的。
➢ 短期而言 ，美元指數面臨下行壓力 。但市場仍持續關
注美國經濟數據及聯準會態度 ，若市場對升息預期再
起，美國2年期殖利率或將重新上行 ，並帶動美元指數
再次走揚。
資料來源：JPMorgan, 2026/8/21(上) / Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/8/27(下)

### Page 10

ECB9月或再升息，市場重點關注通膨數據
10
ECB官員重點關注再通膨風險 圖表解讀
➢ 近期布蘭特原油價格回升 ，加上歐元區 HICP年增率仍
高於ECB中性通膨稅準 (2%)，使部分官員擔憂通膨再
度升溫。若能源價格維持高檔或升溫 ，ECB可能於9月
採取進一步升息行動，避免通膨預期再次失控。
➢ 從歐元區綜合 PMI走勢可見 ，近期重新站上 50榮枯線 ，
顯示經濟活動已重返擴張 ，反映市場對歐洲景氣前景
轉趨樂觀 。然而，當需求回升與勞動市場穩健並存時 ，
企業較容易轉嫁成本壓力 ，也成近期通膨升溫的因素 。
➢ ECB理事會成員 Schnabel於8/26表示，隨中東衝突持
續、歐元區經濟走強 ，使通膨有上行風險 ，故利率必
須進一步上升 。短期而言 ，歐元兌美元因美元走弱而
偏強，而歐洲央行再次升息將使美歐利差進一步收斂 ，
讓美元指數延續偏弱震盪格局。
資料來源： Bloomberg/北富銀整理, 2026/8/25

### Page 11

長天期殖利率回落有助投等債表現
11
債市表現說明
➢ 美國公債： 美國公債殖利率因財政部干預自高檔回落 ，
但長天期殖利率仍有可能受財政赤字及期限溢價影響而
維持高檔 ，而短端利率則因聯準會短期可能不會有動作
而相對有下滑空間。
➢ 全球投等債： 隨長天期殖利率回落 ，美國投等債短期表
現強勁 ，但非投等債近月表現仍優於投等債 ，顯示市場
對經濟軟著陸及企業獲利前景保持樂觀 。隨著違約風險
維持低檔 、信用利差持續收斂 ，資金開始由公債轉向追
求更高收益的信用資產 。不過由於利差已接近歷史低位 ，
後續表現將更依賴企業基本面支撐。
➢ 新興市場當地債： 新興市場當地債為近月表現最亮眼的
債券類別 ，主要受惠美元走弱 、資金回流新興市場及部
分國家貨幣升值帶來的匯兌收益 。隨著全球資金風險偏
好改善 ，新興市場債不僅享有較高殖利率 ，也成為追求
收益與資本利得的重要配置方向。

### Page 12

公債殖利率陡峭化，主因仍是財政赤字
12
8/7以來，全球公債殖利率曲線陡峭化
圖表解讀
➢ 近期全球公債殖利率曲線逐漸由倒掛回歸陡峭 ，主因
並非單一央行政策 ，而是市場重新評估長期成長與通
膨前景 。各國財政赤字擴大 、政府發債規模增加 ，以
及AI資料中心 、能源轉型與國防支出等長期投資需求
上升，使市場要求更高的長天期資金報酬率。
➢ 在殖利率曲線陡峭化下 ，長天期債券價格面臨較大壓
力，因此宜降低存續期間 ，優先配置短中天期債券 、
浮動利率債券或階梯式債券組合 ，以降低長端殖利率
上升造成的資本損失。
➢ 目前市場預期除澳洲外 ，多數已開發國家央行年底前
仍不排除進一步升息可能 。升息本身並非殖利率曲線
陡峭化的直接原因 ，而是市場預期經濟成長 、通膨與
資金需求維持強勁後所產生的結果。
資料來源： JPMorgan, 2026/8/21

### Page 13

圖表解讀
➢ 據小摩統計，若將標普500企業以「未償債務總額」分
組，全數組別在2026Q2財報都繳出超過20%的盈餘年
增率，顯示美國大型企業獲利動能持續強勁 。逐組來
看，動能最強的是「未償債務總額超過500億」的組別，
也是債務最龐大的組別 。而動能最弱的則是未償債務
「50~100億」及「50億以下」的組別，部分解釋為何
近期大型科技企業持續增發新債，市場仍熱情買單。
➢ 然而，以個別企業CDS分析並以甲骨文為例 ，目前其5
年CDS價格已來到 223.7bps，不僅遠遠高於其餘大型
雲 端 供 應 商 水 準， 也 已 遠 高 於5 年 非 投 等 債CDS 的
107.9bps，顯示市場仍會對甲骨文未來自由現金流長
期流出感到擔憂 ，並對公司預期 2030年將再次轉正的
預測感到質疑，因而導致CDS價格持續走高。
➢ 因此，建議在買進投資級科技債時 ，仍應慎選標的或
以投資組合進行，避免承擔過多個別企業風險。

### Page 14

弱勢美元帶動資金進駐新興市場
14
股市表現說明
➢ 台灣加權指數： 台股近月上漲 5%，表現仍優於多數成熟
市場，主要受惠於AI伺服器、先進製程及半導體供應鏈需
求持續強勁 。國際資金回流亞洲科技股 ，加上新台幣升
值預期，有助提升外資配置意願。
➢ 越南指數：越南指數過去一個月大漲9.13%，為表現相對
亮眼的市場之一 ，反映國際資金加速流入東南亞高成長
市場。受惠於製造業轉移 、內需消費擴張及外資持續布
局，越南股市成為新興市場中的焦點標的 。在美元轉弱
與全球降息預期升溫背景下 ，高成長、高β值市場往往更
容易吸引資金追逐，也帶動越南股市領漲亞洲市場。
➢ 標普金融指數： 近期美國殖利率曲線趨於陡峭 ，有助改
善銀行淨利差(NIM)與獲利能力。同時市場對景氣軟著陸
預期升溫 ，也降低金融機構信用風險 。金融股轉強不僅
代表產業基本面改善 ，更象徵本輪漲勢開始呈現健康的
輪動擴散格局。

及半導體設備需求同步攀升 。台灣掌握先進晶圓代工
核心地位 ，韓國主導高階記憶體市場 ，日本則在半導
體設備與材料領域具備深厚優勢 。三國分工明確且具
技術護城河 ，在AI基礎建設持續擴張下 ，可望共同分
享新一輪產業成長紅利。
➢ 近期高盛再度上修全球半導體產業 CAPEX預測，反映
市場對未來3年AI需求保持高度信心 。由於全球新增投
資多集中於先進製程 、記憶體與設備領域 ，最直接受
惠的正是台灣 、日本與韓國產業鏈 。隨著產能利用率
回升、企業獲利改善及資金持續流入科技產業 ，台日
韓半導體產業將成為未來數年全球投資重要核心資產 。
➢ 短期而言 ，持續觀察各國公債殖利率走勢 ，並確認市
場對高殖利率不利成長股折現現值的態度 。然而，長
期持續看好AI發展之硬體需求及整體產業前景。

### Page 16

圖表解讀
➢ 中國半導體產業鏈正處於新一輪擴產週期 ，2030年前
投資維持雙位數成長 。無論是晶圓製造 、設備材料或
IC設計，政策支持與國產替代需求皆提供長期動能 。
雖然目前研發費用與全球龍頭企業仍有差距 ，但基期
低、成長空間大 ，未來有望吸收成熟產能與市占率 ，
帶動獲利同步提升。
➢ 其中，科創50指數被視為 「中國版費半指數 」，成分
股涵蓋半導體設備 、晶片設計 、EDA軟體、先進材料
及高端製造等戰略產業 ，不僅反映中國科技自主化趨
勢，也代表國家資源持續向創新產業集中。
➢ 然而，科創50短期或面臨獲利兌現速度不如預期 、估
值偏高及市場風險偏好下降等挑戰 。此外，美中科技
限制及部分企業仍處於高研發投入階段 ，可能造成獲
利波動與股價震盪，投資表現短期存在不確定性。

### Page 17

➢ 近期美股產業輪動現象明顯 ，健護、能源與原物料位
於相對強勢區域 ，短、中期股價動能最強 。市場風險
偏好仍維持高檔 ，但資金開始由過去高度集中的 AI概
念股，逐步擴散至更多受惠經濟成長與景氣循環的產
業，使整體多頭結構更加健康且具有延續性。

的個股股價低於 200日均線 ，優於標普 500指數平均
(26.3%)，顯示產業內多數個股同步上漲 ，而非僅靠少
數龍頭撐盤 。當市場廣度持續改善 ，通常意味著資金
參與度提升 ，有助於延長多頭行情週期 ，也反映投資
人對經濟前景仍保持相當程度的信心。
➢ 整體而言 ，科技股仍是市場重點關注焦點 ，但短期股
市由健護 、金融、能源及原物料等產業強勢帶領 ，顯
示美股資金輪動趨勢強勁 。建議投資人應多元分散布
局，在科技股為核心下 ，伴隨金融 、健護、能源、原
物料等產業作短期衛星配置。

### Page 18

圖表解讀
➢ 儘管利率維持高檔 ，美國消費市場仍展現強勁韌性 。
就業市場穩定 、薪資持續成長 ，加上資產價格維持高
位，帶動家庭消費支出保持擴張 。從零售 、旅遊到服
務性消費皆維持正向成長 ，高收入族群受惠於股市與
房地產增值所產生的財富效果 ，消費意願明顯優於歷
史平均水準，成為支撐美國經濟的重要力量。
➢ 隨著相關成本上升 ，中低收入族群購車能力受到壓縮 ，
而受惠於金融資產增值的富裕家庭則持續貢獻主要需
求。因此，富人經濟已成為許多產業維持成長的核心
驅動力，也是觀察美國消費趨勢的重要指標。
➢ 作為非必需消費代表企業 ，特斯拉正積極推進旗下自
駕計程車生態布局 。隨著監管逐步開放 ，Robotaxi有
望持續擴大城市覆蓋率 ，提升車輛使用效率與平台價
值。若未來自動駕駛商業化順利 ，將開啟新的成長曲
線，進一步受惠高端消費與科技升級趨勢。

股票
市場
美國 美股上漲動能反映風險偏好情緒。半導體和大型科技股領漲，正向的財報結果，顯示AI交易回來了。
企業獲利續支撐股市，市場焦點轉向企業基本面與AI資本支出，雲端、半導體是主要受惠族群。
中國 中國政策仍偏寬鬆，A股表現較好的集中在科技硬體，例如科創板50指數。官方積極推動增量措施引
導長線資金入市，預期增量政策發酵，資金回補落後與科技類股，帶動類股輪動與反彈格局。
新亞 AI伺服器、高速傳輸、ASIC、先進封裝及散熱供應鏈仍為市場主流，記憶體晶片價格回升與 AI 需求
拉動。亞洲科技股展望依舊正向，不過短期估值已高，留意韓國科技股波動及槓桿比率是否再起。
科技 全球AI投資循環仍處於擴張階段，大型科技公司持續增加CapEx，帶動半導體、網通、光通訊及資料
中心基建需求，市場資金偏好具有獲利成長能見度的龍頭企業。
非必需
消費
美國就業仍具韌性，消費力維持穩定，惟高利率下消費者更重視品牌及價格。Amazon、Costco、

Netflix等龍頭企業財報表現仍優於預期，建議聚焦具品牌力、富人經濟及高定價能力企業。
原材料
/能源
中東衝突、俄羅斯煉能減損及歐美老舊煉廠關閉，原油供給危機引發成品油荒，煉油裂解價差與煉油
利潤衝上歷史高位。汽油價差亦創近年新高，推升石油巨頭的煉油部門獲利至歷史罕見水準。
債券
市場
投等債 8月中旬發行的谷歌55億澳元債及QTS 39億美元債皆獲市場大力支持，認購倍數分別高達3.3/6.0倍，
即便殖利率相對偏高，但仍反映市場資金對高評等企業債需求強勁。
非投等債 非投等債具高息收與低存續期，整體結構已出現質變，不再由低評級「垃圾債」主導。美國非投等債
到期高峰為2029年，短期將不會有在高利率下的再融資壓力。
匯率
市場 美元 美國財政部擴大「債券回購」規模使美元供給增加並走弱，持續關注美國經濟數據及未來利率走向，
若升息機率再次走升或有望支撐美元短期跌勢，但市場普遍看好美元將在2027年持續走弱。

歐洲 經濟維持低度成長，企業獲利改善速度較美國溫和，焦點回歸企業基本面與財政支出。國防、工業
自動化、電網升級及能源轉型仍為中長期投資主軸，持續關注歐洲通膨與ECB對再次升息的態度。
日本 東證持續推動提升股價淨值比與資本效率改革，吸引國際與本地資金流入。日本薪資增長，使非必
需消費與內需企業基本面改善。AI與全球半導體供應鏈需求旺盛，挹注相關科技類股營運動能。
健護
醫療
高齡化社會推動長期慢性病、癌症治療及長照等剛性需求。減肥藥市場需求超預期，帶動大型藥廠
獲利成長。政策推動藥價監管，製藥廠獲利率承壓。大廠暢銷藥品專利到期，將面臨競爭加劇風險。
公用
事業
公用事業類股因數據中心帶來的龐大電力需求，已從傳統的防禦型「收息股」轉型為兼具穩健配息
與成長潛力的標的。這類公司具備強大護城河，但高利率環境估值壓力較大。偏防守與收益型配置。
金融 高利率環境讓銀行能擴大借款成本與放款利息之間的利差，帶來豐厚營收。投行業務與交易活動大
增。但消費者拖欠率上升，以及民間信貸市場面臨貸款損失壓力，銀行與券商面臨潛在呆帳風險。

房地產 美國30年期房貸利率升至近一年新高，購房需求持續承壓。住宅市場仍受到高房貸利率限制，醫療
保健不動產投資信託受益人口老化的剛性需求，相對較為看好。
工業 美國再工業化持續，中美戰略競爭迫使美國決策者重新審視工業化的必要性，工業股維持中性配置。
債券
市場
公債 全球政府財政紀律備受質疑，公債殖利率持續走揚，隨多國或再次升息壓制通膨，短期公債或續跌。
新興
市場
新興市場債殖利率相比全球債券及美國債券更具吸引力；許多新興市場央行已早早升息並使通膨回
落，本地貨幣債實際殖利率在全球中相當突出，但需謹慎選擇，因匯率波動與政治風險不可輕忽。
